**2026-07-27 merge digest (the LOOP system — shipped at last, with APPROVE and REJECT made real):** `claude/missing-looping-system-6e26e6` -> `feat/harness-backend` no-ff merge `a82f301a` (snapshot/reset point `1a9aacfd`; post-merge claims re-lock `7d5c047d`). Gate **test:fast 407 green ON TRUNK** and **test:http 42/42 suites green ON TRUNK**, claims planning authority **PASS** (184 locked paths, +2 for the LOOPS frontend). `sidecar/index.js` and `package.json` are both in the diff, so both gates were owed and both were run — on the lane at `eb832655` and again on trunk after the merge. **No `shared/` change, no credential moved.** Andrew's report: *"we added some whole looping system with UI. I noticed it's not even in the new update."* He was right, and nothing had been lost — `claude/starnet-loop-system-345398` (24 commits, LOOPS window included) was **finished 2026-07-25 02:02 and simply never merged**, so it missed the v0.6.7 cut structurally. Its merge-base is `716f68bb`, which is trunk's head on 07-24 — the day the v0.6.7 scope evaluation ran and froze a candidate list the loop lane appears on neither side of, because it was still being built. It sat **436 commits behind** while eight other lanes merged around it. Trunk was merged INTO the lane first and gated there; three conflicts, all trivial (a comment-only line in `sidecar/index.js`, a `test:http:raw` union in `package.json` with trunk's order canonical, and `claims.json` taken from trunk and recomputed). Trunk then advanced 10 more commits mid-work, so the final merge re-ran with exactly ONE conflict — `claims.json` again — and `frontend/index.html` auto-merged with **both** sides verified present (the LOOPS dock button AND the composer placeholder-width fix).
- **THE FEATURE WAS 80% OF A FEATURE AND THE MISSING 20% WAS THE PART IT IS NAMED AFTER.** The review gate ran **no git at all**: `loopjob-driver`'s `defaultHarvest` returned `commit: null`, no `harvest` dep was ever injected in `index.js` (zero grep hits), and `handleLoopsVerdict` only recorded a row and re-armed the loop. So an iteration's edits piled up UNCOMMITTED in the Commander's project — **approve promoted nothing, and reject undid nothing.** The row said rejected while the code sat exactly where the agent left it. `loopjob-store`'s own header had already specified the missing half: *"the HOST performs the actual git ... BEFORE calling this — the store records what happened, it never claims an apply it did not witness."* New `sidecar/loopgit.js` is the pure decision (branch, pathspec, undo plan); `loopHarvest`/`loopUndoWork` in `index.js` are the git, the same split `nightpatch.js` uses.
- **LAW — NEVER `git add -A` IN A HARVEST.** A loop starts in a tree that may already hold the Commander's own uncommitted work. A pass stages only after-MINUS-before (the `before` snapshot the driver already computes for the check, now passed to the harvest too), so their work is never folded into a commit a later rejection reverts. Proven live: an untracked `MY-NOTES.txt` is absent from the loop's commit and survives an undo byte-intact.
- **LAW — UNDO IS A REVERT, NEVER A RESET, AND IT RUNS NEWEST FIRST.** Rejecting #3 must undo the #4/#5 the STACKING LAW discards; reverting the oldest first conflicts on any file a later pass touched again. Revert keeps every original commit reachable, so a mis-click is recoverable — "never destroy the last copy" applies to the Commander's repo too.
- **LAW — THE DIRTY-TREE PRECONDITION IS SCOPED, NOT GLOBAL.** `nightpatch` refuses on any dirty tree, and copying that here reads as safe and is useless: one scratch file would disable REJECT forever on a real repo. The undo refuses only when a file **in the blast radius** (the union of paths the doomed commits touched) is currently dirty, and the failure recovery restores only those same paths.
- **⛔ THE REGRESSION THIS LANE ALMOST SHIPPED — THE HARVEST LAUNDERED REWARD HACKS.** S2's law is *"TRUST IS THE FULL UNCOMMITTED STATE"* — are the check files in a state git has not recorded. **Committing every pass destroys that question's premise.** An agent weakens a test, the harvest commits it seconds later, the working tree goes clean, and the NEXT pass's green reads as **trusted**. Tampering only had to survive one settlement to become invisible — precisely the hole S2 was written to close, re-opened from the other side. Caught by `loops-check.e2e` going red inside `test:http`, never by any unit test. Fixed by moving the baseline from the index to `loop.baseCommit` (captured before the branch is cut): `loopTrustSurface` unions the uncommitted set with everything the loop's own branch changed since. The two questions are deliberately kept apart — *"what did this pass touch"* (the review card) stays on the working tree; *"can this green be believed"* spans the harvest's commits.
- **A TEST THAT CANNOT FAIL PROVES NOTHING — and this one couldn't, twice.** `loops-git.e2e`'s tamper phase passed with the fix reverted until two defects in the TEST were fixed. **(1)** A mock that re-tampers on every pass keeps the tree dirty forever, so the old index-based guard keeps catching it; the hack must happen ONCE and then stop. **(2)** `iterationCount` rises when a pass **STARTS**, so waiting on it hands back the PREVIOUS iteration's verdict while the new one is still in flight — the wait must key on `lastCheck.n`. Only after both does reverting the fix produce 4 red assertions naming the laundered file. **Generalize: before trusting a regression test, revert the fix and watch it go red.**
- **Two more real bugs the change exposed, both fixed.** A loop with **no** project folder was being blocked from rejecting at all (research loops — the common shape), because "nothing was committed" was conflated with "cannot undo"; the plan is now four-valued and `unrevertable` (a real folder that is not a git repo) still records the verdict while stating out loud that the files were left in place. And two loops sharing one folder would now fight over the checked-out branch, so `loopPrecheck` stands the second one down by name until the first finishes its pass.
- **Proof instruments.** `test/loops-git.e2e.test.js` (new, 55 assertions) drives a REAL sidecar against a REAL throwaway repo and asserts **on the repo, not on the API's account of it**; `test/loopgit.test.js` (new, 68 pure assertions). `commit` now also rides the `recent[]` projection — an approved iteration previously lost all trace of where its work went.
- **NOT verified, stated plainly.** **No live browser walk of the LOOPS window was done** — everything above is proven through real sidecars and real git repos, not eyes on the UI, and the window itself arrives exactly as the original lane left it. The verdict toast's new wording (reverted-count, `undoNote`, the approved branch name) is code-read only. `test/loops.e2e.test.js`'s *"a dormant loop spends NOTHING"* assertion **flaked once under `test:http` load** and could not be reproduced in 10 standalone runs, nor attributed to this lane (4/4 green at the pre-harvest merge commit); the money guard itself is closed deterministically in the pure gate (`loopjob.js:157`, a dormant loop is `enabled:false` and stays bound a day later), so the flake is a snapshot-timing artifact in the test, not a spending loop — **but it is unexplained and owed.** The harvest also cannot distinguish an edit the Commander makes to their own project WHILE a pass is running from the agent's, and commits it; that is the same attribution the tamper guard has always used and there is no signal that separates them. And the loop system still has only ONE real-model dogfood run behind it, from the original lane.
- **REAPING OWED:** worktree `.claude/worktrees/orchestrator-agents-architecture-d22dd6` and branch `claude/missing-looping-system-6e26e6` are merged and clean but **NOT reaped** — the merging session's shell cwd is inside that worktree (the phantom-worktree trap). Reap from outside: `git worktree remove <path>` then `git branch -d claude/missing-looping-system-6e26e6` (`-d` only). The source branch `claude/starnet-loop-system-345398` is now fully contained in trunk and may be reaped the same way.

**2026-07-27 merge digest (wall crown RING — the walls stop reading as "only a back wall"):** `claude/wall-rendering-appearance-6e2494` -> `feat/harness-backend` **fast-forward** to `a53f3c86` (snapshot/reset point `e717ef0d`; code `22780940`/`2891342b`/`734582f8`/`e94c34a9`/`4e1b1d4d`/`53d08ddc`/`a8d65e68`, in-lane trunk merge `7c6de7ed`, post-merge claims re-lock `a53f3c86`). Gate **test:fast 402 green ON TRUNK**, claims planning authority **PASS** (182 locked paths). Frontend-only, so no `test:http` owed; **no hotfile, no `shared/`, no `package.json`, no credential moved** — the diff is exactly `frontend/app/stationbake.js`, `frontend/app/crtlab.js`, their two website mirrors, `dev/wallprobe.mjs` (new), `.gitignore` and `claims.json`. Trunk had advanced **64 commits**, so trunk was merged INTO the lane first and gated there (402 green) before the fast-forward; exactly ONE file conflicted — `qa/product-perfect/claims.json`, the expected re-lock collision — resolved to trunk and recomputed at the merge result. Andrew's report: the walls "cut off as if there's only a back wall", and "on the left and right side, there doesn't really feel like there's a real wall there". Seven review rounds, all Andrew-verified, final sign-off "you have perfectly nailed it" plus a live multi-room test.
- **LAW — IN THIS PROJECTION A WALL IS ITS LIT TOP SURFACE.** Only the north wall is extruded up-screen, so it was the only one whose HEIGHT could be read — and the only one with a crown. The e/w straights painted 3px of `U.shade(base,-0.22)`, **darker than the deck they enclose**, fronting 9px of global near-black hull, and the chamfer sweep deliberately tapered the crown to zero at the side end. So the bright line that defines a wall at any zoom died at both top corners. All of e/w/s now paint the same ladder `bakeTallNorthFace` paints, read OUTWARD: contact seam (darkest) - inner face - dark under-seam - CROWN - lit outer edge - hull.
- **LAW — THE CROWN'S WIDTH IS CAPPED BY `pad` (7).** `buildLightMap`'s ambient plate is `footprint + pad`; a crown wider than that hangs OUTSIDE the mask and renders at its RAW baked tone against the starfield. `WALL.sideCap` 5 leaves 1px of margin.
- **LAW — `WALL.side` MUST EQUAL `pad`.** It was 12: the hull plate, its rounded corners and its riveted rim all stop exactly `pad` out, so the band was a slab jutting 5px past the station's own silhouette. **Invisible while the band was near-black, obvious the instant it got a crown** — a pre-existing defect that a brightness change EXPOSES rather than causes. The crtlab `side` slider max and the three WALL presets were clamped with it.
- **LAW — NEVER EASE AN OUTLINE; A SHEARED CIRCLE IS NOT A CIRCLE.** Two corner rounds were rejected before this landed. First the chamfer carried TWO outer profiles (a flat ring on the hull curve, plus a separate column sweep rising across only the DECK curve's 12 columns while the hull reaches `pad` further out) — the outline stepped inward then kinked onto a steeper staircase; **two curves meeting at an angle is not a corner, it is a notch.** Then one profile but displaced by a per-column EASED amount, which is a shear: curvature piles up where the ease is steepest, and Andrew drew the arc he wanted over a screenshot — it "needs to perfectly curve at the top left and the top right the same way it does on the bottom". A RIGID TRANSLATION PRESERVES A CIRCLE, and it is what is physically true (a wall's top surface IS the footprint ring moved up-screen by its height). The outer boundary is now the same radius-HR quarter circle the bottom corners use, centre moved to `cy`, and the region it vacates is wall FACE. **`cy` is DERIVED FROM BOTH TANGENTS AT ONCE** — `centre = (the straight north wall's outline top) + HR` puts the arc's topmost point on that line with a horizontal tangent AND its leftmost point at `ax - HR = X - pad`, exactly the e/w wall's outer edge, with a vertical tangent. True for any `WALL.up`. `eachTallCornerCol` is deleted; do not reintroduce a second profile.
- **LAW — A DISPLACED RASTER MUST PAINT THE EXTENT IT OWNS, NOT ONE PIXEL.** Shifting a row-walk by a per-row amount skips rows wherever the lift outruns the walk's index: output rows ran 35, 37, 39, 41, 42, 44 for six consecutive source rows, so 36/38/40/43 were never written and the outline rendered as a **COMB**.
- **LAW — THE CROWN EASES ONLY TOWARD A WALL THAT IS ACTUALLY EXTRUDED.** `crownEase` ran capW->capH around EVERY corner; a BOTTOM corner joins two walls both read from straight above, so both ends are capW and the taper put a **5 -> 3 -> 5 waist** at exactly the corner. Measured bare, polar: 3px at 45 degrees between 5px straights; a flat 5px now.
- **LAW — HALF-OPEN VS INCLUSIVE, AND ONLY ON THE +VE SIDES.** A plate spans `[c-pad, c+pad)` so its last painted row is `c+pad-1`, while `round(centre + HR)` is the first pixel PAST the art. West/north already agree; east/south sat 1px proud and the whole ladder shifted with them, so a bottom corner met the south crown one row low. The hull rim walk had always carried the `ex - 1` correction. Lit-edge row across the join went `5 5 5 6 6 6 6 5 5` -> a monotone ramp into 5.
- **LAW — THE UNMASKED HULL SKIRT OUT-SHINES ANY INTERIOR SURFACE UNLESS THE AMBIENT GIVES WAY.** The skirt hangs in void and is deliberately outside the ambient plate: flat luma 58. The crown takes the full 0.77 ambient and drops to 38 at a room's dark end, so **the shell was brighter than the lit top of the wall inside it** — and that, not geometry, is why the bottom corners still read faintly once exact. **Brightening the baked tone CANNOT fix it: through 0.77 ambient even a pure-white crown tops out near luma 62.** The crown painter records its rects (`crownRects`, the `viewportRects` contract) and `buildLightMap` pulls ambient back over exactly those pixels — MULTIPLICATIVE, so the crown still falls off away from the lamps, hard-edged because it stands for a surface not a light. `LIGHT.crown` 0.45 + crtlab slider; **0 restores the old inversion exactly.** Dimmest crown pixel anywhere 38 -> 86 against a brightest-skirt 58.
- **LEAK-CHECK CORNER WORK ON A MULTI-ROOM STATION.** Andrew, on his own build: "these lines for some reason, these shadows that are randomly scattered". `crownReach` (the corner crown's reach, which the mask pass reads back) was ONE flat map keyed by absolute bake COLUMN. **That is indistinguishable from correct on a one-room station, because no two chamfers there can share a column.** Stack rooms and they do: the mask pass then ran from a FAR-AWAY corner's reach down to THIS corner's tile, laying a tall 1px column of ambient over bare space — invisible against black, plain against the starfield. Keyed per chamfer now. **Every leak check this lane ran scored 0 and every one was on the seed's single room.** 148 leaked px -> 0 on a stacked layout. Extends the magenta-composite law: the right composite is not enough if the FIXTURE cannot express the bug.
- **CRT LAB drift caught at merge, pre-existing and live.** `RESET` does `Object.assign(wall(), WALL_DEFAULTS)`, so that object is not the readout's key list — it is written back over the live bake config. It still said `up 9 / side 12` against a shipped `14 / 7`, so RESET restored a state that never shipped and `side 12` pushed the band past the hull. The three WALL presets carried the same stale side. `sideCap`/`crown` had sliders but were missing from the key sets, so COPY VALUES silently dropped them. `WALL_DEFAULTS` now mirrors `StationBake.WALL` exactly — **add a WALL knob, add it here.**
- **MEASUREMENT LAWS (each paid for itself in one round).** **Judge SHAPE on a BARE composite (`baseCv`, no light map) and TONE on the lit one** — the corners sit at the room's darkest end where ambient crushes the ring into the skirt; the 5->3 waist was invisible lit and obvious bare. **The JOIN TEST: for each column across a corner into its straight, which row carries the lit edge — a clean join is a flat run of one number.** **The game canvas draws at a fractional uiZoom, so scanning a screenshot measures a RESAMPLE** — the first measurement of this lane was meaningless for that reason. **Magenta must be EXCLUDED from a max()** or void scores 105 and every GAP reads as a peak. Proof instrument `dev/wallprobe.mjs` rides along (offscreen 1:1 bake in a real browser, magenta composite, per-layer leak count, ring/arc/join scans, bare + lit crops); `SKYNET_PROBE_MULTI=1` builds the stacked four-room fixture.
- **GOLDENS FLAGGED FOUR FRAMES AND NONE OF THEM ARE THIS LANE'S — NOT BLESSED.** `ingame` 5.06, `work-tasks` 2.86, `work-deliverables` 4.40, `sys-notifs` 3.47 (thr 1.5). A control run on the merged tree with ONLY this lane's four files reverted to pre-merge trunk flags **the same four**: 4.44 / 2.80 / 4.07 / 3.19. So all four were already over threshold on clean trunk, and this lane adds +0.62 / +0.06 / +0.33 / +0.28 on top — the station genuinely changed in `ingame`, and the panel frames move only because the world renders behind them. Blessing would have silently baselined another lane's unreviewed surface, so it was not done. **The drift is owed by whichever lane last touched those panes.**
- **NOT verified, stated plainly.** Corridor-only corners were never exercised: `corUp` is 0, so a corridor's top corners take the flat/bottom path, and no probe fixture pairs a corridor chamfer with a lifted one. Doorways on an e/w wall (where the crown must break for a threshold) were not measured. A repainted room's crown follows the room's wall hue by construction but was only checked on the default palette. Andrew tested a real multi-room station live and signed off; the corridor and doorway cases rest on code reading, not pixels.
- **REAPING OWED:** worktree `.claude/worktrees/wall-rendering-appearance-6e2494` and branch `claude/wall-rendering-appearance-6e2494` are **merged and clean but NOT reaped** — the merging session's own shell cwd is inside that worktree, and removing it mid-session is the phantom-worktree trap (a failed `git worktree remove` deregisters FIRST and deletes SECOND, leaving a `.git`-less orphan whose git commands hit the MAIN repo). Reap from a shell outside it: `git worktree remove <path>` then `git branch -d claude/wall-rendering-appearance-6e2494` (`-d` only; if git refuses, it is not merged — stop).

- **⛔ TRUNK WAS ALREADY RED GOING IN, and still is.** The Green Guardian's last cycle (2026-07-27 21:08Z @ `5e7b2d46`) is RED on one open **P1**, `a735d29f`: "Truth regression: audit assertion `log/frozen-bus` failed (floor-rest)" filed at trunk `7af7a1de` — the UI showed something the harness did not do. **It is unrelated to this lane** (event bus / audit log; this diff is rail flex, transcript rendering and the CRT warp) and is NOT closed by this merge. The Guardian's own STATUS row was sitting uncommitted in the integration tree when this merge began and is committed alongside this digest rather than discarded — it is real data and not this lane's to drop.
- **⛔ THE APERTURE IS THREE STACKED LAYERS AND THE CSS ONE IS THE WEAKEST — tune in this order or waste a round.** Andrew circled the feed and said the curvature was costing him screen. The first pass tuned the CSS glass (`#stage-wrap::before`) and he reported, correctly, *"i feel or see no difference."* It could not have worked. **(1)** The warp's inverse `ro = rs(1 - k*rs^2)` has a maximum reach of `(2/3)/sqrt(3k)` — only **1.283 at k=0.09** — while a rect corner sits at `sqrt(2) = 1.414`. Those pixels had **no source**, so `buildLUT` wrote `-1` and both render paths hard-filled them. Running the shipped LUT algorithm over a 1288x882 frame: **22.26% of the panel was literal black.** That is a CROP shaped like a rounded oval, not a vignette, and it is what Andrew was drawing around. **(2)** The in-canvas vignette `1 - 0.55*r^2` then cut the edge midpoints to **45%** brightness and clamped the corners to **zero**. **(3)** Only then does the CSS glass composite — on top of pixels that are already black, where softening it changes nothing. Fixed by making both in-canvas constants knobs (`CRT.vig`, `CRT.over`) rather than by touching curvature: **`vig 0.55 -> 0.30`, `over 1.00 -> 1.20`**. Measured on the same frame: hard-black **22.26% -> 0.11%**, edge-midpoint brightness **0.45 -> 0.79**, corner **0.00 -> 0.58**. `over 1.24` reaches exactly 0.00%. Cost is ~20% of edge content cropped, which is the zoom-in Andrew explicitly asked for. **`curve` stays 0.09 throughout — he said "i dont really want to change the curvature level", and it was re-read as 0.09 after every preset.**
- **PARITY LAW for anything added to the warp:** `vig`/`over` had to be wired identically into the GL uniforms AND `drawCurveCPU`'s LUT and its radial-gradient stops (alpha at gradient fraction t = `vig*2t^2/over^2`), because `drawCurveGL`'s `probeMeans` compares the two paths and defects to CPU on divergence. **And `over` MUST be in the `_lutKey`** — without it, dragging the slider serves a stale LUT and the CPU path silently stops matching the GPU one.
- **COMMS HAS TWO STOP PATHS AND THE INTERRUPT USES THE OTHER ONE.** A resolved run card printing `RUN STOPPED` was followed by a work-log line reading `stopped` — the same word, one row down, with less detail. The `endReason` branch was fixed first and **did nothing**, because a Commander interrupt unwinds through the `catch(e)` `stopped` branch, which called `toolLine('⏹ stopped')` immediately before the `resolvePresence` that prints the headline. Only a real interrupted run against `dev/seed-mock-comms` caught it; the code read correct both times. The informative stop reasons (step limit, budget door, an unnamed `endReason`) still print — only the exact restatement is gone.
- **A pre-run control surface must retire when its run does.** The `my read` brief card kept full height forever: `briefReadCard`'s `retire()` was only ever reachable from `send()`, so after the run ended the assumption chips still read "tap to correct" and the steer box still looked live while `/api/run/steer` had nothing to steer. It now folds to caption + objective at run end (**310px -> 70px**, measured live), disables the chips, replaces the input with an honest closed note, and reopens on click — nothing deleted, same fold idiom as the resolved run line above it.
- **The rail was giving all its slack to the wrong list.** `#crew` held `flex:1` while `#ws-wrap` held none, so the roster absorbed the column and `#workstreams` — the list that actually grows without bound — sat capped at `max-height:40vh`. Measured at 1920x1030 with 8 real sessions and one agent: crew **513px for a 44px row**, sessions **251px**. After: crew **44px**, sessions **719px**, reaching 1px off the rail floor. **LAW: the unbounded list gets the flex; the roster is content-sized.**
- Three smaller ones. The jump-to-latest pill covered the newest transcript row at **every** reachable scroll position (there was no position where you could read the line you were scrolling back to); `#chat-log.pill-clear` now reserves its height, verified 47px clear at the bottom-most unstuck position. `render()` in `widgets.js` had been stamping `data-empty` on each rail since it shipped and **no CSS ever consumed it**, so an empty widget rail was a `--ph-faint` glyph on a transparent border — an entire pinnable surface nobody could see. And the crew head counted "N AGENTS" one panel above `#crew-sum`'s "0 WORKING / 2 IDLE", which totals the same roster and says more.

The create screen and the Bay were converted to hold their handles — the module-level `SkinStage.show()` targets whichever stage mounted LAST and is now only a compat shim. **2026-07-27 merge digest (COMMS sound board — the leftover cues):** `claude/comms-sound-design-ae2ab1` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `b5a467d2` (snapshot `b98e1643`; code `8d65cea7`/`e7de85b7`/`7067d6c7`/`aa31eb91`, post-merge claims re-lock `494f1575`). Gate **test:fast 402 green ON TRUNK**. Frontend-only, so no `test:http` owed; no hotfile, no `shared/`, no `package.json`, no credential moved. Exactly ONE file overlapped trunk's 34 intervening commits — `qa/product-perfect/claims.json`, the expected re-lock collision, resolved to trunk and recomputed at the merge result because neither side's lock described the merged tree. **User report: COMMS still sounded like the old sound board.** It did. When the licensed Interface Bleeps pack landed 2026-07-19 only 10 of its 36 files were mapped, and the five cues left on the pre-pack synth were `type`/`quest`/`level`/`milestone`/`sale` — four of which are exactly what COMMS fires. `type()` was the loud one: it ticks under EVERY revealed COMMS line (`Chat.typeLine`, used by onboarding/intake/tutorial) at ~20/sec. The buttons were always on the new board; the beats never were. **⛔ LAW EARNED: never synthesize or compose a cue — use a pack file.** The first pass re-voiced the stings by pitch-shifting `notify` into an arpeggio (`_arp()`) and was rejected on sight: layering an existing sample IS making the sound. The pack already held what was needed. All six cues now play their own unmodified original at rate 1.0 — `type`=Click_03 74ms (the pack's shortest, the only reason it survives 20/sec), `tick`=Data_Point_05, `sale`=Confirm_01, `quest`=Confirm_02, `milestone`=Sequence_07, `level`=Sequence_06 — the celebration tier graded by the pack's own lengths (235ms < 843ms < 1572ms), mapping chosen by Andrew off a standalone audition build. **Two silences went with it.** `type()` stamped `_lastAny` on every tick and `audio.js` yields 80ms after any stamp, so at 20/sec the yield window never closed and EVERY button pressed while a line typed itself out was mute; ticks are ambience, not interaction cues, so they gate quietly now. The model-dock toggle stops propagation, so the delegated cue never reached it and it had none of its own. **Two cue names were referenced but never existed**, and the `sfx(n)` helper's `SFX[n] &&` guard swallowed both: `sfx('tick')` at eight `connectors.js` sites (the whole window changed state in total silence) and `SFX.error` on the failed-restore path, now `bad()`. **A loudness inversion was caught by measurement, not by ear:** Sequence_06 is recorded ~12dB below the other two picks (raw peaks -17.1 vs -4.4/-5.3 dBFS), so the LONGEST sting rendered the QUIETEST and a level-up landed smaller than a milestone — playback gain 2.0 (file untouched; the originals stay unmodified) puts it at -19.8 against milestone's -19.3, with 0 clipped samples at SFX.vol 0.35/0.7/1.0. **LAW: pack files are not level-matched — render a graded set through an OfflineAudioContext copy of the master chain and compare peak/RMS; it is the one audio judgement that does not need ears.** NOT verified: whether a 1572ms level-up collides with whatever COMMS prints next, which needs a real XP threshold crossing.

**2026-07-27 merge digest (Hermes memory-parity: the four gaps):** `claude/starnet-hermes-memory-gaps-408646` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `df7ae537` (snapshot `92a6f2a6`; code `a1fa9fdb`/`37059546`/`a4a72bcb`/`bd5ae899`, post-merge claims re-lock `b0610fc2`). Gate **test:fast 401 green + test:http green ON TRUNK**, claims planning authority **PASS** (181 locked paths, path set unchanged). Trunk was baselined **400 green at `92a6f2a6` BEFORE the merge**. Only ONE file overlapped trunk's 9 intervening commits — `qa/product-perfect/claims.json`, the expected re-lock collision, resolved to trunk and recomputed at the merge result because neither side's lock described the merged tree. No hotfile in my diff (trunk's `build.js`/`worldmodel.js`/`stationbake.js` changes are disjoint from mine and were `node --check`ed anyway), no `shared/`, no routes beyond one additive GET, no `package.json`, no credential moved. NOTE: another session merged the CONTROL FLOOR lane (`0ad324c1`) on top of this one mid-gate, which is why the step count reads 401; the first post-merge gate run showed a `spotify.store.test` D1 failure that did NOT reproduce in isolation and did NOT reproduce on a clean re-run — two concurrent `test:fast` runs in one integration tree race on shared scratch paths. **Closes the four gaps from the code-level re-audit against the reference harness.** (1) **recall_conversation only ever searched the OPEN workstream** — `transcriptstore.search()` hard-filtered one streamId, so an agent asked what was decided in another workstream answered "no messages match" while the answer sat on disk one stream over. It takes `scope:'all'` now, every hit carries its own streamId so a cross-stream quote is attributable, and two shapes reach parity with the reference harness's session search: `streams()` browses the workstreams that exist with a topic preview, `around(streamId, ts)` reads the turns surrounding a hit. **LAW: the anchor must be the TIMESTAMP** — a row index or synthetic id dies when `trimStreamRam()` splices rows out from under a drill-down, which is exactly when a drill-down is attempted. The tool infers its shape from its args and **AUTO-WIDENS a zero-hit stream search**, saying so; an explicitly pinned `scope:'stream'` never widens, because answering "we never discussed that" off a one-stream miss is the confidently-wrong claim this project treats as a defect. (2) **notebook.write was never dupe-checked** (reflection deduped its PROPOSALS by Jaccard since M-mem.6; the direct write path did not), so the same belief accumulated in a dozen phrasings, diluting top-K recall and leaving the Commander a panel of noise. The reference harness forces the issue with a hard char cap that ERRORS when full; we have no cap by design, so the equivalent is to stop the redundant write. **It CHALLENGES rather than blocks, and the reason is a real limit: token overlap cannot separate a restatement from two different facts sharing a shape** — "staging db is in frankfurt" vs "production db is in frankfurt" scores as high as a genuine paraphrase, because the one token carrying the difference is the one that differs. Any threshold tight enough to catch paraphrases will sometimes swallow a real memory, and a false suppression is worse than a duplicate. So the agent is shown the belief it already holds in full and decides: a restatement stops there, a genuinely distinct fact returns with `distinct:true` and saves. A challenged write **emits nothing** — no memory.write, no deliverable — because a receipt for a write that did not happen is a false claim. (3) **memory only formed on the WATCHED browser run.** `reflect:true` was set at exactly one call site, so cron fires, night shifts, workshop shifts, channel messages and the local OpenAI-compatible endpoint all did real work and threw away everything they learned. The rationale in the code ("only the WATCHED browser run reflects -> a turn-in beat") was true when reflection always raised a BLOCKING deck and stopped being true at the silent-save reversal. **The flag never meant "attended", it meant "this run did real work"** — it is set at every real-work host now. **Delegated workers deliberately stay OFF:** worker output returns as tool results inside the PARENT's messages, so the parent's reflection already covers them, and per-worker would pay N aux calls for one task and race N batches into one deck. Spend is bounded exactly as before (AuxGovernor budget, per-agent cooldown, salience gate all sit downstream of the flag). (4) Two things had to be true first. **ORIGIN:** records were keyed `notebook:<agent>` with no surface dimension, fine while every record was self-evidently the Commander's own conversation. `memcore.originOf` stamps `commander | channel:<name> | schedule | nightshift | api` at both write paths and the Memory Core shows a chip for everything except `commander` — the ordinary case stays quiet or the label becomes noise nobody reads. Channel DMs are owner-gated upstream (`adapter.js ownerOk`), so the real exposure was a whitelisted group chat. An untagged legacy record projects as `commander`, its only possible source. **DURABLE PENDING:** high-stakes proposals waited in an in-memory Map that died on restart and was reachable only by exact runId or a single newest-per-agent fallback, so a deck raised at 3am was a belief the Commander never got to rule on. `pending:<agent>` queues them durably with provenance (cap 50 FIFO, idempotent per `runId+":"+id` since every batch mints a prop_1), `GET /api/memory/pending` lists them across runs, a verdict arriving after a restart resolves against the QUEUE instead of 404-ing a real decision, and the new-hero reset wipes it so a re-commissioned hero inherits no stranger's deck. Live-verified on a booted seed station across a sidecar restart: origin chips render, the pending section shows the deck, **Keep drained the queue to 0, hid the section, took stored 3 -> 4, and landed the record on disk with `origin:'schedule'`, trust 0.3, and the sourceRunId of the run that PROPOSED it**. NOT verified: a real model calling recall_conversation cross-stream, and an unattended run reflecting end-to-end with a live provider — both need a key. **STILL OPEN, deliberately:** recall_conversation's reach is the RAM mirror (~1200 turns/stream via `ramPerStream`); older dialogue is on disk but unsearchable, where the reference harness searches its full SQLite DB via FTS5. Also **one source-lock law earned**: `sidecar.http.test.js` pinned the literal `if (prop.saved) return json(409` and broke on a rename while the guarantee was intact — **a needle must pin the GUARANTEE, not the variable name holding it**. Rides the next cut.

**2026-07-27 merge digest (connector approval asked on EVERY call):** `claude/approval-popup-repeated-33e63d` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `418a26a4` (snapshot `7af7a1de`; fix commit `1e6d85f5`). - **The bug (user video):** an MCP agent asked for approval on every connector call — four popups for four `get_draft_asset` reads while the user clicked "Full access" each time. `impact:'external-unknown'` routes connector tools to the run-authority gate, which prompts per call, collapses once/session/always/full to a bare boolean, then stamps `oneShot:true` — and `registry.dispatch` skips the consent broker whenever that is set. The broker is the only thing that records a grant, so nothing was ever written.
- **Second symptom, same cause:** an agent with `approvalMode:'full'` **in settings** was ALSO asked on every connector call (proven 3 calls -> 3 popups before, 0 after). The authority gate is deliberately blind to Full Access; the bypass lives in the broker tier it was skipping. Ordinary tools always honored the setting.
- Watched connector calls now route to the broker, narrowed by `isConnectorTool` (`mcp:<id>`) exactly as the unattended lane narrows its grant — the external-unknown catch-all keeps its exact per-call confirmation. Connector defs are now unconditionally `requiresConsent:true`; a remote server's own `readOnlyHint` must not be able to switch off its own gate now that the broker owns the ask.
- Proven live over real HTTP in `e2e.mcp-connector`: reverting the two source files turns "exactly ONE popup" into **got 4** — the video, reproduced. "Always" lands as a revocable grant; a deny still refuses and is never cached as a yes; the ungranted autonomous floor is unmoved.
- **OPEN, deliberately not fixed here:** "Full access" on ANY card writes `'*'`, so one click on a *connector* card also unlocks `shell.exec` and every other connector for that agent (probed). That blast radius is pre-existing on every other card — but until this merge, connector cards were accidentally exempt, and they are the highest-frequency card an MCP agent raises. Narrowing `full` on a connector prompt to a connectors-scoped token is the recommended follow-up; it is a product-semantics call, left to Andrew.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (branding notice — MIT code, StarNet brand reserved):** `claude/license-terms-revision-514a09` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `ae2c0eef` (snapshot `178be72d`; another session's `cb935e97` FOR YOU fix landed between snapshot and merge — disjoint files, gated together here). Correction `6bb5850d`, re-lock `d777ecbb`. Gate **test:fast 400 green ON TRUNK**, claims planning authority **PASS**; `qa:product-perfect` still exits 2 on the one pre-existing `installed: last smoke was BLOCKED`.
- **The lane could not ship as-authored.** Its README paragraph said forks were permitted "for noncommercial use" — residue from the PolyForm relicense (`72f4fc32`) that was reverted the same day (`280fc4dd`) — which MISSTATES our own licence, because MIT permits commercial redistribution. Merging it would have put a false statement of StarNet's licence terms in the README of a public repo, at a release.
- Corrected wording now says the true thing in all three places a redistributor looks: the code is MIT and may be forked, modified and sold; the **StarNet** name, logo, station artwork and sprites, and the rest of the brand identity are owned by Andrew Sims and are NOT licensed with it, expressly or by implication; a fork must ship under its own name and must not present itself as StarNet or as endorsed by it.
- **NOTICE.md gains the same reservation** (new final section) — beyond the lane's scope, and deliberate: NOTICE.md is the file a redistributor is expected to carry, where README is the first thing a fork rewrites.
- **LICENSE deliberately left as pure MIT.** Appending a branding clause to it would risk GitHub classifying the repo as "Other" instead of MIT, which is exactly the open-source credibility the MIT choice was made for. The reservation lives in README + TERMS §7 + NOTICE.md instead.
- Surface re-lock: README.md 7883 -> 8532, TERMS.md 4761 -> 5309. No claim locator needle sits in the License sections, so none moved. `grep -i "noncommercial|non-commercial|source-available|polyform"` across README/TERMS/LICENSE/NOTICE/PRIVACY/INSTALL/DOWNLOAD_PAGE returns **zero**.

**2026-07-27 merge digest (CONTROL FLOOR — no white HTML controls):** snapshot `92a6f2a6`; merge `0ad324c1`. Gate **test:fast 401 green ON TRUNK** post-merge. Root cause was structural, not local: app.css skinned controls **per surface** (`.btn` / `.bb` / `.mkt-in` / `.fbc-sel` / `.mp-model` / `.key-input`) with **no element-level floor underneath**, so any control missing its class fell through to the user agent — `.key-input` on a `<select>` sets flex/font-size/padding and none of the paint. The visible deltas are 2 white selects (`#tg-bot-agent`, `#dl-status`) becoming phosphor fields, 5 OS-blue accents becoming phosphor, and 200 buttons leaving Arial for the station font stack; the rest are no-ops on invisible surfaces (border-colour on zero-width borders, text colour on empty icon buttons, radius on borderless elements). Post-merge live sweep: **0 offenders / 875 controls**, no console errors.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (orchestration audit — the overseer→crew delegation path):** `claude/starnet-orchestration-audit-c22cd0` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `93777905` (snapshot `10d837d0`; the prop lane's `df4431c7` landed between snapshot and merge, so the real reset point was `df4431c7` — the two diffs share zero files). Gate **test:fast 400 green + test:http exit 0 ON TRUNK**, `orchestration.test` 116 -> **169 assertions**. Diff is `sidecar/tools/builtin/orchestration.js` + 11 lines of host wiring in `sidecar/index.js` + the test; no `shared/`, no routes, no `package.json`, no credential moved. `orchestration.js` is a hotfile, so it got the ritual re-check anyway (`node --check` + symbol uniqueness: `workerWire`/`workerSystem`/`waveSize`/`postureNote`/`childAbort`/`abort`/`lastAssistant`/`resumeRunnerFor`/`makeOrchestrationTools` each exactly once; the repeated `leadId`/`overflowRows`/`childEmit` are per-tool-run locals in distinct `run()` scopes, not auto-merge duplicates). **The audit found eight things and 230 assertions were already green — every one of these bugs lived in the gaps BETWEEN the tests**, in how the parts compose rather than in any part. **The headline: a sequential `team.dispatch` that ran long lost EVERYTHING.** The only clock over the whole call was the registry's tool timeout, so one slow worker ate the shared budget, `withTimeout` rejected, and the `out` array — every row already collected — was discarded; the lead received the string `"team.dispatch timed out"` and nothing else, after the Commander had paid for all of them. The shipped ceiling made it reachable rather than theoretical: 20 min total (`CAPS.maxIters` x `toolTimeoutMs`) against 4 sequential workers each allowed 10 x 30s of tool time = exactly 20 min, **with zero headroom for model latency, which is most of the wall clock and appears nowhere in that formula**. LAW: **a wall clock derived from tool-timeout arithmetic is not a wall clock**, and **the mechanism that enforces a deadline must be the one that can still return partial work** — a registry-level timeout can only throw the results away. Each foreground worker now gets its own slice on its OWN abort controller chained to the parent (E-STOP still cascades, proven against a real `AbortController`), so a straggler is stopped ALONE and comes back as one honest `timeout` row carrying its partial text plus "do not present it as complete", while its siblings' results survive; sequential shares divide what is LEFT so a fast early worker donates its unused time, and the registry timeout is demoted to a backstop a minute above the budget. **Second: a parallel dispatch of 4 ran TWO at the shipped defaults.** The lead holds an admission slot for the entire dispatch and `MAX_CONCURRENT_AGENTS` is 3, so the gate refused the last two workers and **nothing ever retried them** — `maxWorkers` (4) simply exceeded the default fan-out capacity (2), and the lead synthesized a confident answer from half the crew's work. LAW: **a fan-out width that exceeds the admission gate's capacity is a silent-loss bug, not a queue** — refusal must never be the resting state of a requested worker. Both modes are one wave loop now (sequential = waves of 1, unchanged default; parallel = waves sized to the gate's LIVE free capacity, wired in from the host), a refusal from a race for the last slot is retried exactly once (a refused worker did no work and cost nothing), and a surviving refusal names `MAX_CONCURRENT_AGENTS` instead of blaming the model. Measured against the real gate at cap 3: **4 requested -> 4 done, 0 refused, gate peak 3** (was 2 done / 2 refused). **Third: `slice(0, maxWorkers)` dropped subtasks 5+ in silence** — 6 workers in, `"dispatched 4 worker(s), 4 done"` out, so the lead reported on work that never ran; the overflow now returns explicit `not-dispatched` rows naming each skipped worker and the summary says so. **Fourth: the delegated worker's APPROVAL clause was the wrong one.** A worker shares the LEAD's consent broker, but its prompt carried the clause composed from its OWN roster `approvalMode` — an engineer the Commander had set to FULL ACCESS, dispatched by an ask-mode overseer, was told "never wait for a go-ahead" while every write actually paused, and **that per-agent toggle silently did nothing for delegated work**. LAW: **truthful telemetry binds the PROMPT too** — if the host will not honor what the identity claims, the identity must not claim it; the effective posture is appended last and explicitly supersedes it. Also confirmed UP TO PAR and not worth re-auditing: a dispatched crew member gets its full station identity byte-identical to a direct COMMS run (`composeSystemPrompt` -> roster push -> `workerSystem`, dossier and standing orders included), blessed project roots are station-wide so "go work in my repo" genuinely reaches the worker, the summon->dispatch roster race is closed by `await lastRosterPush`, flat depth holds (a clone gets no orchestrator object), the 20s hold-open heartbeat keeps a long dispatch from being socket-killed, and forwarded `agent.cost` keeps the COMMS total honest even when the work is later lost. **STILL OPEN (unfixed, ranked):** (1) **a delegating run's per-run $ cap bounds nothing** — child spend never returns to the lead's `spentUsd` and every cross-run pool has shipped at 0/ungoverned since the 2026-07-23 legibility pass, so a "$10" run can cause ~$160 of delegated spend, and the comments in `concurrency.js:3` / `index.js:353` / `orchestration.js:11` that justify the fan-out with "the day/global pools already cap aggregate $" are **STALE**; needs Andrew's call on where a dispatch-tree ceiling lives. (2) `team.resume` on a `team.spawn` clone always fails (an ephemeral `sub-<uuid>` is never in the roster by design), answers `resumed`/ok:true anyway, and **overwrites the record's kept partial result and zeroes its usd** — while LIVE HELPERS' STOP tooltip promises exactly that resume. (3) a finished background subagent's result has **no UI surface at all** (the `task{kind:'subagent'}` event is consumed only by the world sprite ledger; LIVE HELPERS queries `?status=running`; the lead is never re-woken) — deliverable FILES do surface, the report text does not. (4) no dedup on duplicate agentIds in one parallel dispatch, and delegating to a crew member the Commander is actively chatting with waits 45s on the workspace lease then fails "workspace busy". (5) the subagent registry's in-memory `records` is never trimmed (only the disk write slices to `keep`). Detail + probes: memory `orchestration-audit-2026-07-26`.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (poker table, six prop fixes, three tables):** `claude/poker-table-props-polish-6735cf` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `df4431c7` (snapshot `10d837d0`), in-branch claims re-locks `16bf9f4c` / `356e24cb` / `b4dbed3b`. Gate **test:fast 400 green ON TRUNK**, claims planning authority PASS (181 locked surface files, **zero drift** vs the merged tree). Frontend art + placement only — no sidecar/routes/shared/package.json, and **zero file overlap with trunk's 14 intervening commits**, so the merge was clean; the hotfiles in the diff (`worldmodel.js`, `build.js`) were re-checked anyway per the ritual (`node --check` + symbol-uniqueness: checkProp/addProp/moveProp/setPropRules/migrate/commitPropStamp/propCardHTML each exactly once) and the catalog now maps **1:1 onto the draw functions in both directions**. **The poker table read as a monitor on a grey mic stand**, and the four causes are each a reusable law. (1) The padded rail roll was 8px deep on a 48px table, so FELT — the one thing that says "card table" — survived only as a slot; it is a 4px perpendicular offset off a true racetrack capsule now. (2) The pedestal used `U.shade(rail, +0.46)`: **lightening a NEAR-BLACK desaturates it toward grey**, which is literally where the brushed-steel stand came from — dark materials need authored lit tones, never a large positive shade(). (3) Five near-white board cards, two seat stacks, a five-colour chip tray and four pot stacks all landed inside the middle 20px and fused into one pale block; **adjacent per-item LINE outlines butt together into a continuous black bar**, so the board became a filmstrip — the dark felt is already the separator. (4) Nothing carried the table's edge thickness, so the oval hovered. Also STATIC now: no dealer, no motor, no emitter means the v4 river-flip clock, blinking mid-toss chip and pulsing "ambient" had no mechanism ([[prop-animation-honesty-lane]]). **Six more props read as the WRONG OBJECT**, found by rendering every family and looking: `monstera` hung from the overhead so its cords terminated MID-AIR (this projection draws no ceiling) with container above and foliage below — a box with a green beard, now a floor planter; `hazardpad` painted its stripes onto BARE DECK with an alternating band within two values of the floor, so only every other stripe rendered over nothing (and its band parity came from `(i / step)` while the loop starts negative, so parity only worked while the step divided h — a counter now); `cans` packed three cans plus three outlines into one 12px tile; `arc_floorlight` was a slab of neutral #dfe2e0 plus two blooms, i.e. a white pill; `dartboard` had dull-gold beds on near-black; `cablerun` drew two of three strands within a couple of values of the deck. **THREE TABLES** (side 1x1 / lounge 2x1 / long 3x1) close a real catalog gap — hero surfaces existed, nothing in between, so every small object was parked on the deck where it reads as litter. LAW: **every table presents its top plane at the SAME height (`SURFACE_RISE`)**, which is the only reason a `mount:'surface'` prop can be seated by one constant instead of a per-table lookup; author a new table to that plane or everything on it floats. The model does NOT import the catalog — `WorldModel.setPropRules()` injects the lookup **at MODULE level, not per instance** (a per-instance setter must be re-armed at every create/deserialize/undo-clone site, and the missed one places props illegally), and with no rules installed behaviour is byte-identical to before. Mount is resolved **PER FRAME, never stored on the prop**: reclaim the table and the prop drops back to the deck instead of floating, so **no saved station needs migrating**. **ELEVEN props retired** (graffiti, zapper, cans + the eight wall-only ones) — and retiring a type is NOT just deleting its row: `draw()` no-ops on an unknown type but the doc entry keeps its FOOTPRINT, so a saved station would carry an **invisible obstacle agents path around forever**; `deserialize` prunes unknown types now, and `world.js AGENT_DECOR` lost `cans`/`poster` because an agent picks its own tile and so cannot own a prop with a placement rule. **WALL MOUNTING WAS BUILT ON THIS BRANCH AND REMOVED AGAIN ON IT** after Andrew rejected the look on sight ("I hated everything you did with this wall mounting"); the history keeps both commits, the tree keeps neither, and the `mount:'wall'` rule was deleted rather than left dormant — a placement constraint with no props subject to it is dead code you still have to reason about, and a dormant rule invites the next prop onto it. **v3 law #5 (wall-style props are freestanding) therefore STANDS.** Verified beyond the gate: a browser-free recording-ctx probe over all 108 props x idle/working x 12 timestamps (no throws, no NaN, alpha balanced, `U.shade` in range, >=30% of paint inside every footprint, and surface props seated EXACTLY on their footprint bottom), 32 model assertions on the placement rules, and an in-situ capture on a live seeded station with the props placed through the real validated `addProp` path. Catalog **116 -> 108**. Rides the next cut.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (deferral reliability, measured):** `claude/starnet-hermes-parity-gaps-99c415` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `58f3bb1a` (snapshot `25e6dab4`). Gate **test:fast 400 green ON TRUNK**. Follow-up to the tool-schema lane, driven entirely by measurement against REAL models on OpenRouter. **The prompt now NAMES the deferred tools instead of counting them** — "N more tools are not shown" asks the model to first imagine a capability might exist and only then look for it, and that imagining step was the actual barrier; ~400 B of names standing in for ~8.5KB of schemas, riding the CACHED prefix. **Two probe flaws found and fixed first, which matters more than the result:** the HTTP-status task everyone reaches for is GUESSABLE (example.com obviously returns 200) *and* has a CORE alternative (`web_request` returns a status), so "did not call browser_network" conflated three different things and produced a false exoneration of deferral one turn earlier. The clean probe is a **viewport resize** — no other granted tool can do it and there is nothing to guess, so a claim without a call is unambiguous fabrication. **Result on the clean probe:** naming recovered `claude-haiku-4.5` and `gpt-4.1`, taking the panel to **4/6** models that find and use a tool they were never shown (sonnet-4.5, haiku-4.5, gpt-4.1, gemini-2.5-flash). **HONEST RESIDUAL:** it does NOT fix the weakest tier — `gpt-4o-mini` claims it resized without calling the tool, `llama-3.3-70b` silently skips the step, and **both use the tool immediately with `SKYNET_TOOL_SEARCH=0`**, so deferral genuinely costs capability on 2/6 and produces a fabricated claim on 1/6. That is the documented reason the kill switch exists, and the reason to hold the memory/skills deferral idea. **The risk is BOUNDED to the specialist tail:** headline capabilities are core and **9/9 models took a real screenshot and reported it honestly**, so a user asking to see something always gets it. LAW reaffirmed with numbers: **never defer a headline capability**, and **a byte saving that buys a fabricated result is not a saving**. Rides the next cut.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (tool-schema cost lane):** `claude/starnet-hermes-parity-gaps-99c415` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `ef522fa3` (snapshot `2b40689b`). Gate **test:fast 400 green + test:http green ON TRUNK**. No frontend/shared/package.json in the diff -> no claims re-lock, no golden. **The lane started from a measurement, not a hunch:** booting the real sidecar against a scripted mock and reading the wire showed a fully placed floor advertises **72 tools = 37.7KB = 59.7% of EVERY request**, re-sent on every turn (~1.5MB at maxIters=40) — and it was NOT mostly the browser (memory/skills averaged ~900 B/tool, routines/quests ~1,161). Ended at **53 tools / 29.7KB**. Four levers, in the order the measurement justified them. **(1) Prefix caching** `a7022b67`: Anthropic renders tools -> system -> messages and caches by PREFIX, so ONE `cache_control` breakpoint on the last system block covers the entire tool catalogue in front of it; a second rides the last message block. `cache_control` had been requested in exactly one place (openrouter's system message) while all four adapters READ `cached_tokens` back — a statistic we never asked for. **LAW THIS CREATES: the system prompt is now a CACHED PREFIX.** It works only because the prefix is byte-identical all run (loop.js resolves `tools` ONCE; `extractLeadingSystem` hoists only the LEADING system run, so mid-run `<steering_note>`/`<loop_guard>` land as USER turns) — **adding a clock, a turn counter or a remaining-budget line to the system prompt silently converts a 0.1x discount into a flat 1.25x surcharge, with no error.** Proven by driving the REAL loop 5 turns and diffing prefix bytes, with `<loop_guard>` observed riding the messages. **(2) The coupled honesty fix** `7d949555`, and it is not optional: `prices.js` had warned since it was written that folding cache traffic into prompt_tokens means "a heavily-cached run OVER-reports" — free advice while nothing requested caching. Measured once it did: **3.19x over-report, ~$1.62 of phantom spend per 40-turn run**, so the budget seatbelt fired that early. Cache ratios now ride the price object (anthropic 0.10x read / 1.25x write, gemini 0.25x); **a family with no entry keeps 1.0x so it can only ever OVER-report, never under** — the safe direction for a spend cap. Discount applies to DOLLARS ONLY; `prompt_tokens` stays the true count because the context gauges read it. **(3) tool.search** `aa0203bd`: a CAP_REGISTRY grant row may carry `deferred: true` — still GRANTED, just not advertised; the loop appends what the search finds for the rest of the run. **DEFERRAL IS ADVERTISING, NEVER CAPABILITY** — `resolveTools` still returns deferred tools in `tools`, so gate/consent/kill-switch are untouched, and `attenuate()` filters `deferred` by the same subset or a delegated worker could FIND what it may not run. Rides the `computer` object (the quest.update precedent) with capId `toolsearch`; never itself deferred. **TWO bugs only a live run against the real sidecar could see:** it fell through `impactOfTool` to EXTERNAL_UNKNOWN and the authority layer refused it, making every deferred tool unreachable while looking merely "not found" (**the SIXTH registration site is `SAFE_BUILTIN_CAPS`** — identical to the July `taskbrief` failure); and index.js renames dotted names to underscores BEFORE the loop sees them, so the reveal map was keyed `browser_screenshot` while the signal carried `browser.screenshot` and every lookup missed **silently** — the unit test passed because it skipped the rename. **(4) The finding that changed the design** `0cc21db0`: everything above used a scripted mock TOLD to search, which proves machinery and nothing about behaviour. Against REAL models — sonnet-4.5 searched and succeeded, **gpt-4.1-mini never searched and then REPORTED TAKING A SCREENSHOT IT NEVER TOOK**, gemini-2.5-flash said the capability did not exist. A/B with deferral off proved the change caused it. Fixes: the system prompt now states the list is **PARTIAL, with a count** (a model plans from the tool LIST, never from the description of a tool it has not decided to call — the one place it will never look; this alone fixed gemini); browser.screenshot + browser.vision returned to core; `SKYNET_TOOL_SEARCH=0` kill switch. **LAW: never defer a HEADLINE capability — a byte saving that buys a fabricated result is not a saving.** Specialist tail re-checked and fine. **(5) Phase 4** `276bcbe3` closes the last two Hermes gaps: ANSI stripped at the single exit of `runCommand` (shell.exec + the bg tail share one strip; the regex is built with `String.fromCharCode` so the source carries neither a literal ESC nor a backslash — it compiled to an unterminated group twice first), and a **central tool-output cap** in registry.js `okResult`/`errResult`, the one seam every result passes, so a NEW tool no longer inherits zero protection (80k, `SKYNET_TOOL_OUTPUT_MAX`, above the per-tool clamps; keeps HEAD **AND TAIL** because the exit line lives at the end, and the note names a next action because "truncated" alone invites the identical retry). **Description trimming is CLOSED, finished not abandoned:** measured across all 51 advertised tools, a description is only safely trimmable where the SCHEMA documents its own fields; every remaining long one (notebook_write, quest_update, team_dispatch, fs_search, todo) sits on a BARE schema, so the prose is its parameters' only documentation and cutting it buys bytes by degrading tool SELECTION. ~200 B of safe upside left. Do not reopen by ranking on total bytes — that list is dominated by SCHEMA size (web_request is 1,277 B total, 438 B of description). Three kill switches ship with the lane: `SKYNET_ANTHROPIC_CACHE=0`, `SKYNET_TOOL_SEARCH=0`, `SKYNET_TOOL_OUTPUT_MAX`. Rides the next cut.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (prop animation honesty):** `claude/unnecessary-prop-animations-f8776c` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `5b1db217` (snapshot `b54657ff`), in-branch claims re-lock `17ada174`. Gate **test:fast 399 green + test:http green ON TRUNK**. Frontend art only (no sidecar/routes/shared/package.json); zero file overlap with trunk's browser lane, so the merge was clean. **The user report:** a screenshot of a potted plant with a cyan light blinking above it — "some of the props have very unnecessary animations… makes no sense." **Root cause is systemic, not a one-off:** `propsprites.js` is littered with `// (kept)` markers, the fingerprint of the [[prop-upgrade-v2]] sweep whose charter was "all animations/live-states preserved". Nine parallel agents carried every v7 blinker into the new art without anyone asking whether it made sense, so **110 of 117 draw functions were time-animated** and `(kept)` is NOT evidence a behaviour was ever wanted. The catalog's `animated: true` flag is useless as a filter — every prop carries it. **The rule applied:** an animated pixel must answer *what mechanism moved it*, and only three answers are legal here — real harness telemetry, the object's own mechanism running, or real ambient physics. The sharp form, which is why this is an honesty bug and not a taste one: **a blinking emissive IS the station's telemetry vocabulary** (it is how the connector portal, workbench and cap props report real state), so spending it on scenery both lies about the object and dilutes the lamps that carry true signals. Second class: **a specular cannot travel on a bolted-down prop** — the object and the ceiling strip are both fixed, so a highlight sliding along it is physically impossible (`cans`, `arc_ladder`, `parcels`). 16 props stripped: the tall plant's floating bud, two "moisture probes", the terrarium lid lamp, a souvenir rocket with a lit cabin AND firing thrusters, a pulsing cork dartboard, a strobing star-chart backlight, per-bin shelf blinkers on staggered 700-970ms clocks, breathing locker name-tags, a pulsing bloom on printed paper, shimmering dried spray paint, a breathing reading lamp, and the three travelling glints. KEPT: motion that IS the object (lava lamp, plasma globe, zapper, holopet, fish, screens, neon, flytrap snap), real ambient physics (foliage sway, the bonsai's falling leaf), and every telemetry-driven prop. **Verified without the game canvas** (screenshots time out there): a recording-ctx probe logs every draw call + property write, renders each prop at 12 timestamps spanning 8s, and diffs the streams — stricter than pixel-diffing because it catches alpha-only changes, and it needs only `global.U` with `shade`+`hash`. Confirmed independently in real headless Chrome via a filmstrip whose frames are compared by `toDataURL()`. Animated props **106 -> 96**; 10 of the 16 are now byte-identical across every frame, the other 6 retain only their intended motion. Both registered in CAP_REGISTRY - the tool-parity guard would have failed otherwise, which is exactly why it exists. **2026-07-25 merge digest (agent sprite smoothness):** `claude/agent-sprite-smoothness-981a49` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `ce47789b` (snapshot `3751582f`), claims re-lock `ee7152ef`. Gate **test:fast 395 green ON TRUNK** + **GOLDEN PASS on trunk (17/17 within threshold, no re-bless owed)**. Frontend-only (no sidecar/routes/shared/package.json) -> no test:http.

**The user report:** agent sprites look "very 2D and not very smooth when they turn around", hypothesis "maybe we are lacking in frames". The hypothesis was WRONG and worth recording: frame count (6/dir) was never the problem, and the fix required no new art. Five causes, all in the movement/draw path: (1) `drawBody` rounded the body to integer WORLD units while the camera scales 0.5-6x, so a body sat still ~2 frames then hopped 2-6 DEVICE pixels — the loudest artefact, and note panX/panY were never rounded so the world was already sub-pixel while the body alone snapped; (2) facing was a bare `|dx|>|dy|` snap recomputed every frame, DUPLICATED IN THREE PLACES, so it strobed on near-45 headings and teleported the pose on a turn; (3) the walk cycle ran on the wall clock at fixed fps although pace is not fixed (crew temperament 0.88-1.17x, hero 34 u/s vs crew 28), so feet skated; (4) bodies went 0 -> full pace in one frame; (5) `path()` is a 4-NEIGHBOUR BFS, so every route was an orthogonal staircase and bodies **never moved diagonally at all**.

**Shipped:** device-pixel snap via `ctx.getTransform().a`; one `stepGait()` replacing all three facing snaps (continuous angle slewed at `TURN_RATE=12` rad/s, bucketed with `DIR_HYST=0.13` hysteresis); distance-phased stride on a `b.odo` odometer; speed easing (`ACCEL=150`) that brakes into the final stop only; and string-pulled paths in `worldmodel.js` behind a conservative `losClear` (every touched tile walkable, every orthogonal hop `canStep`-legal, diagonals demanding BOTH corners so a shortcut can never squeeze between two blockers). Waypoints also hand over `CORNER_LOOK=2.5` early and NO LONGER snap px/py onto the waypoint — that snap WAS the pivot.

**Two traps this lane paid for.** `b.phase` is DUAL-PURPOSE: `phaseOf()` indexes `PHASES[]` with it, so making it a float to de-sync the walk cycle returned `undefined` and killed the entire idle/mood engine on frame 1 — the float sprite offset is now a separate `aph`. And stride must never be a constant units-per-frame: **ULTRON ships 4 walk frames at 0.60 scale** where the other 38 sets use 6 at 0.36-0.425, so a fixed 3.4 spun his legs ~2.3x too fast; it is now DERIVED (`frameHeight * scale * 0.56 / fr.length`), which also makes any future skin correct with no retuning. Separately: a trailing `//` comment silently swallowed `pathPts/pathIdx/idleUntil/goal/say` off the crew spawn literal and `node --check` still passed — caught only by reading the SERVED file.

**Guard:** `test/path-smoothing.test.js` verifies the smoother INDEPENDENTLY of its own Bresenham walk — it samples every segment densely and asks `walkable()` about the tile under each sample. 1196 paths, 1712 diagonal segments (a 4-neighbour BFS produces zero), 8.1x waypoint compression, 0 wall violations. The gate meta-lint `lint-gate-can-fail` rejected its first version for lacking `A.report()`.

**Scope verified, not assumed:** exactly THREE movement sites exist in the codebase and all three now route through `stepGait`, and `SPRITES.drawBody` has exactly TWO callers (floor + static dossier portrait), so this covers all 39 skins plus ULTRON and future skins inherit it free. NOT automatic: 8-direction art — `DIR_A` has 4 entries and `pick()`'s fallback is 4 dirs. **DON'T buy diagonal art:** only 3 of 39 live sets have 8dir sources (`finn`/`caseyjones`/`pikachu`, the `*_v3` batch) because `_preset/preset.json` locks `n_directions: 4`; regenerating the other 36 is 540-900 generations against 219 remaining and would change their locked look. **OPEN:** Andrew has not yet judged the one real look change — diagonal MOVEMENT rendered with 4-direction art (the Stardew approach). Merge auto-merged cleanly against `c15960e0` (seat-centre); both sides verified present, all symbols defined exactly once.

**2026-07-26 merge digest (turf reads as grass):** `agent/turf-grass-mat` → `feat/harness-backend`, fast-forward to `f5bde64e` (snapshot `7eb53949`), claims re-lock `f5bde64e` carried from the lane and still valid (source `f1294c2b` ≠ HEAD). Gate **test:fast 399 green (exit 0) ON TRUNK**. Frontend-only → no test:http owed. Rides the next cut. Fast-forward, so no auto-merge touched the `worldmodel.js` hotfile; `node --check` + symbol counts verified anyway.

**The ask:** Andrew supplied a grass reference photo — "lets make our grass turf look more like the top left reference".

**The real defect was not the art, it was the RANDOMNESS.** v2 gave every blade an independent random tone off a 5-step ramp, so no pixel belonged to the pixel beside it — which is exactly why a field of grass read as static. Real grass clumps: a patch lights together and the gaps shadow together. Tone now comes from a two-octave value FIELD (~6px tufts over ~2px strands) sampled on ABSOLUTE bake-pixel coords, so neighbours share a tone, tufts continue across tile borders, and chunk↔monolithic parity still holds. Strands are lit one step off THEIR OWN tuft's tone rather than off a global ramp, so a blade always belongs to its clump. This generalises to any organic surface we add next (moss, sand, water, rust): **correlate, don't scatter.**

**Two tuning findings, both invisible without a render:** plain axis-aligned noise cells produced a QUILT OF SQUARES — right clump size, wrong shape, because every cell boundary lined up in both axes (cells are now sheared, x offset by a function of y); and amplitude ±0.20 read as camouflage blotches, legible clumps that stopped belonging to one surface (±0.13 with a dominant mid-tone reads as one lawn).

**Hue was fighting the material, and it was fixed by MEASUREMENT not by eye.** Grass is olive: red near green, blue well under both. `fern` (#2a3f24) is blue-leaning, and since `vivid()` drives the dominant channel hardest its lifts ran toward PURE green. Sampled channel ratios — reference ≈ R/G 0.83 · B/G 0.48; fern 0.62 · 0.51; new **`meadow` (#374024)** 0.83 · 0.50. A first cut at `#37401e` hit 0.83 · **0.41** and read as mustard: the blue channel is what separates grass from straw. TURF now `suggest`s meadow; existing rooms keep the hue they were built with, and HEDGE stays on fern (a bush is not a lawn).

**One deliberate law inversion:** dry blades use `U.shade`. The standing rule is never to `U.shade` anything that must KEEP its hue — a dead blade must LOSE it, and bleached straw is precisely desaturated-and-lighter, the direction `U.shade` travels.

This commit also carries a one-line Guardian dashboard row refresh (GREEN cycle 2026-07-26 06:06Z @ `7eb53949`) that was sitting uncommitted in the integration tree — same benign automated row that has ridden along with previous merges. That cycle is independent confirmation that trunk with the SPINE/BULKHEAD defaults boots and looks right.

**2026-07-25 merge digest (tool-RESULT injection defence):** `claude/routine-scheduled-runs-terminal-d179ff` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `b64a875c` (snapshot `f622620c`). Gate **test:fast 398 green (exit 0) + test:http green (exit 0) ON TRUNK**, all three lanes re-proven live against the merged tree (taint 9/9, grant 14/14, prompt-guard 15/15).

**The gap.** The cron prompt scanner covers text the Commander wrote. It cannot see content the agent FETCHES mid-run — a hostile page, a compromised MCP server, a poisoned upstream result — and a granted routine auto-approves the terminal and connectors. Pattern-matching fetched content is the WRONG tool: it is arbitrary, and legitimate work constantly quotes attack commands (the exact false positive the cron-guard two-tier split exists to avoid).

**1. FENCE** (`sidecar/tools/fence.js`). web_* had fenced since the web lane; **browser page reads and MCP results did NOT** — the two newest untrusted sources arrived raw, and one of them (connectors) is what an unattended routine can now be granted. Extracted the shipped web fence VERBATIM into a shared module and wired browser (snapshot/get_text/console/dialog/vision) + MCP results to it. One marker pair, one scrub string: a second copy drifts, and a drifted scrub is a fence a hostile page can CLOSE. web.js behavior byte-identical (its own suite proves it).

**2. TAINT** (`sidecar/taint.js`). The structural half, because a fence is ADVISORY — it says what the content is, it cannot make the model obey. So the host removes the payoff instead of detecting the payload: once non-Commander content lands in an UNATTENDED run, that run loses `shell.exec`/`verify.run`, credential-spending `web_request`, and connector WRITES for the rest of the run. It does not matter what the injection says; the tools it needs are gone, and there is nothing to evade. **THE USABILITY LINE IS THE DESIGN** — connector READS, further web reads, jailed fs and memory all survive, or a routine whose job is "read three pages from one connector" dies on call two and the feature is worthless. Enforced pre-dispatch AND mirrored in the consent predicates from ONE shared definition, so gate and consent can never disagree. Autonomous only: a watched run has a human and live prompts, and removing powers mid-conversation would be hostile.

**Proven with the real attack**, not a simulation of one: a maximally credulous mock model (it ALWAYS obeys the page) reads hostile MCP text instructing it to run a command. The result arrives fenced, the injected `shell_exec` is BLOCKED, the refusal NAMES `mcp__notes__read_note`, and the command never runs — while a clean granted routine keeps its terminal and a tainted one keeps reading.

**LANE INTERACTION resolved** with the MCP result-cap lane that landed in parallel: the cap governs the SERVER's payload, the fence is host framing added on top (the same split web_fetch has always had), and **fencing happens AFTER clamping** — clamping a fenced string would slice the closing marker off a long result and hand the model an unterminated fence. Their two cap assertions were rewritten to assert the payload is byte-identical INSIDE the fence rather than that total content length equals the cap; the per-run tool-output budget still bounds the fenced total, so the context protection the cap exists for is intact. Browser conflicts combined rather than picked: iframe markers + fence on snapshot, and the vision `saved` note deliberately kept OUTSIDE the fence (host-authored provenance, not page content).

**Behavior change OUTSIDE this lane, flagged deliberately:** `web_request` is both a taint source (capability 'web') and revoked-when-tainted (`external-credentialed`), so on EVERY autonomous surface (cron, night shift, workshop, channels, openai-compat) it is now effectively single-use after any external read. That is intended — the per-key grant is per-KEY not per-HOST, the model picks the URL, so it is the most direct exfil channel — but it is a real reduction in another lane's shipped capability. One line in `allowedWhenTainted` if it needs narrowing.

**v1 boundaries, stated not hidden:** shell output and local fs reads do NOT taint (a granted routine's own `npm test` output would otherwise revoke its next command). A routine could still `cat` a downloaded file. Documented in the module header.

**TRAPS.** `SKYNET_ALLOW_LOOPBACK_FETCH` does not exist — I invented it; web_fetch to loopback is SSRF-refused (correct), so use an MCP connector as the offline taint source. A mock that DEDUPES tool messages fakes a "second connector read failed". Registry error results are prefixed `ERROR: ` so match block markers anywhere, not at index 0. **The interactive exemption could not be proven live headlessly** — web is SSRF-refused on loopback and MCP needs a live per-call confirmation — so it rests on the unit-asserted `surface !== 'interactive'` guard plus the autonomous positive proof. Verified NOT a bug while chasing one: connector tools persist fine across repeated runs (state stays `up`). No claims re-lock owed — the release surface is frontend/docs only (0 sidecar entries).
