Gate **test:fast 395 green (exit 0) ON TRUNK**. Frontend-only (`stationbake.js` + `worldmodel.js` + the generated `website/app` mirror) → no test:http owed. Rides the next cut.

**Shipped:** `plate` and `plating` are replaced as the DEFAULTS by `spine` (deck, on hab + corridor) and `bulkhead` (wall) — both reached with `floorMat`/`wallMat` still null, so stations already built adopt them; both old materials stay in the palette as the classics, so nothing is destroyed and the old look stays choosable. `courses` and `service` ship as two additional pickable wall materials. Andrew picked the winners from rendered candidates; the two losing BULKHEAD variants were deleted, as were the two losing deck candidates (MODULAR, SECTION).

**Two pre-existing bugs this lane surfaced and fixed, both PROVEN pre-existing rather than assumed:**
1. **The corner crown never tapered.** Around a chamfer the wall face eases to zero height, but the crown kept full `capH` plus its `+0.30` lit top edge the whole way — a wall receded to nothing, still capped at full thickness in the brightest tone in the room, stepped along a diagonal. Read as a bright bar ruled across every corner (Andrew: "the thick diagnal lines"). Sampling the chamfer returned byte-identical luma for plating / bulkhead / bulkhead-at-`wallDetail 0`, which is what proved no wall RECIPE drew it.
2. **The ambient mask shadowed bare space at every rounded corner.** `buildLightMap` covers each footprint plus a wall-height strip with a SQUARE corner, while `eraseSpandrel` only cuts the chamfer out of the footprint plate — so mask lay over void outside the hull, invisible against pure black and plain against the SpaceBG (Andrew, from the live app: "make sure its not visible on the outside"). Extends the straight-wall-top law from `9e50bb99` to corners. Measured: pixels where `baseCv` alpha is 0 and `lightCv` alpha is not went **89 → 0** across the WHOLE canvas (all four corners), and checking out `stationbake.js` from before this lane's wall work returned exactly 89 too — so the crown taper neither caused nor widened it.

Both corner fixes derive from ONE new shared walk, `eachTallCornerCol` — the crown painter and the mask now read the same per-column values, so the mask cannot drift above the painted pixels. Per the one-integer-raster law: no second sampling convention at a corner.

**NO GOLDEN BLESS TAKEN, deliberately.** `npm run golden` in the lane flagged exactly one frame, `build-connectors` (diff 1.96, thr 1.5) — and a control run on CLEAN trunk with none of this lane's code flagged `build-connectors` at the identical 1.96. That drift is the connectors lane's unblessed UI (four trunk commits touched those panes after the last bless `3c75c4ef`), so blessing here would have silently baselined another lane's unreviewed surface. This lane's own world frames stayed inside the noise band (`ingame` 0.49 vs trunk 0.39; `build-station` 0.74 vs 0.60) because the new materials are deliberately value-matched — a 64×40 luma signature cannot see them. **Two open items for others:** `build-connectors` still owes a review + bless from its owning lane, and the golden harness is demonstrably blind to material changes.

**W0:** the release-surface re-lock this lane owed is `92b4f49d`; claims test 64/64 standalone on trunk. The wave verdict itself stays BLOCKED on `authority/installed` (installed-app smoke), which is the standing pre-existing block, untouchable by a frontend change.

**Method note worth keeping:** reviewing bakes composited over a flat `#05060c` void hid the mask leak for an entire session — anything painted outside the hull is invisible against a flat dark fill. Composite over magenta (then count non-magenta pixels outside the silhouette) or over a deliberately bright starfield. Also: never judge pixel art at a non-integer scale — a 1.6× room render invented an artifact that was not in the bake and cost a chased "fix".

**2026-07-25 merge digest (routine unattended grants + injection tripwire):** `claude/routine-scheduled-runs-terminal-d179ff` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `1cee6493` (snapshot `993d3b63`), claims re-lock `0b8b8a7c`. Gate **test:fast 394 green (exit 0) + test:http green (exit 0) ON TRUNK**, plus all three live repros re-run against the merged tree (14/14 grant, 15/15 guard, MCP e2e 48).

**The user report:** "whenever I try to run a routine I always get an error", their agent saying "scheduled runs aren't receiving the terminal tools". The diagnosis was CORRECT and the situation was unfixable by the user: `fullOffice()` carries no WORKBENCH and `enforceRunAuthority` stripped `shell.exec`/`verify.run` on every non-interactive surface "even under Full Access", so placing a workbench — the app's own advice — provably could not work. Byte-identical in v0.6.6 and at trunk before this merge.

**Shipped:** per-ROUTINE opt-in grants, `CronJob.unattendedGrants`, default `[]`. `'workbench'` → shell+verify; `'connectors'` → the Commander's connected MCP servers. The grant is read ONLY from the durable job record — never prompt text, model output, Full Access, or a cached `always` — so a model cannot mint one; consent and the wire tool-list read the SAME value, so an offered tool can never be refused downstream.

**SIX gates had to open, not the two the harness-gap audit named.** The audit missed the consent broker: `permissions.js` tier 2.5 EXEC LOCKOUT hard-denies autonomous+execute BEFORE any grant tier, so opening `enforceRunAuthority` alone produced an offered-but-unusable shell ("silence is not consent") — strictly worse than before. New tiers 2.4 (`terminalAutonomy`/`connectorAutonomy`) sit ABOVE the lockout; **that ordering IS the feature — below it they are dead code**, and a test asserts the index ordering. Also required: `fullOffice()` has no WORKBENCH *and* a bay-docked agent's explicit `station` bypasses `composeOffice` entirely, so the grant had to inject objects via new pure `office.stationWithObject` / `stationWithConnectors` or it silently no-oped for exactly the agents with a real room.

**`'connectors'` is narrowed by capability prefix (`^mcp:`)** — `external-unknown` is ALSO the fail-closed default for anything the host cannot classify, so a blanket unlock would have opened that catch-all for every present and future unclassifiable tool. Test asserts an unclassifiable tool stays denied under a connector grant.

**Injection tripwire** (`sidecar/cron-guard.js`, pure, 87-assertion unit suite) — required once granted runs auto-approve these tools. TWO TIERS and collapsing them breaks one: STRICT on the user-authored prompt (command shapes are a smoking gun), LOOSE on the assembled prompt (command shapes DROPPED — skill markdown legitimately quotes them; the reference harness shipped that false positive and it silently killed every job whose skill mentioned `cat .env` in a postmortem). Invisible unicode: hard block strict, SANITIZE loose. Wired at FIVE doors — HTTP create, `/routine` slash, the AGENT `routine.create` closure (a separate path that calls `cronStore` directly), patch/update, and BOTH fire paths (before the capability gate, so a blocked routine costs nothing). A blocked fire is a real FAILED run (`markRun` + `cron.result{reason:'blocked: <id>'}` — free-string, no owned-schema change), never a silent skip.

**Self-grant hole verified CLOSED and tested:** only the Commander's own panel POST may carry grants; `routine.create` and the `/routine` slash action both build literal specs with no passthrough. Test asserts neither file mentions `unattendedGrants` and that the agent tool never spreads caller args.

**Consistency fixes this forced:** `capsummary`'s unattended note hardcoded "shell/terminal, desktop control and live connector tools are unavailable" — a FRESH LIE to the model on granted runs; now derived from the run's real resolved set, and it renders even when `lackCore` is empty. A gated-away tool answered **"unknown tool: shell_exec"** — false (it IS registered, just absent from the wire list, so its name never translated back); that lie is what sent agents hunting for a broken tool instead of a withheld one, immediately before fabricating success. Now a WITHHELD result naming the actual gate, `summary:'withheld'`. Added a REVOKE ACCESS row action — a standing permission with no withdrawal short of deleting the routine was the remaining gap.

**TRAPS hit this lane.** (1) `npm run test:fast | tail` printed a green-looking tail while **exiting 1** — the claims test was red at step 158. Committing a frontend change invalidates the W0 release-surface lock, and the claims authority reads `claims.json` **at the commit**, not the working tree, so the re-lock MUST be its own commit. Always check the exit code, never the tail. (2) Emoji ZWJ detection must resolve surrogate PAIRS — `codePointAt(idx-1)` lands on the low surrogate, so 👨‍👩‍👧 read as non-emoji and every family emoji would have been blocked as hidden text; caught by a smoke case, not by reasoning. (3) `claims.json` conflicted mid-merge (two lanes re-locked it); the resolution is to REGENERATE the surface from the merged tree, not hand-merge either side.

**STILL OPEN — stated plainly:** this scans PROMPT text. It does **not** scan tool RESULTS mid-run, so a connector-granted routine that fetches a hostile page and then acts on it is NOT protected. Neither harness solves that; it needs turn-level provenance. **NOT PUSHED** — the diff touches `website/app/**` (generated mirror), which auto-deploys starnetos.com on push. Rides the next exe cut.


**2026-07-25 digest (prop lane follow-ups — chair legibility + centred seat):** direct-to-trunk `a96fdfe5` / `c15960e0` (+ claims re-locks `002c1678`, and the surface re-stamped again after). Gate GREEN (392). Two Andrew reports off the v4 prop pass. (1) **CHAIR** — the renderer draws it at EVERY agent seat, so it is the highest-multiplicity prop in the game; v4 gave it material but left its silhouette a slab (dark cap / teal band / dark base), which at 12px reads as a small appliance rather than a seat. Fixed by SHAPE not brightness — a waisted backrest (headrest bar clear of the shoulders, pinched lumbar) plus ARMRESTS breaking the outline east/west, drawn last so they sit in front of the pad. Zero emissives added: the quiet contract that keeps it from competing with the workstation is intact. Same commit repaired **26 mojibake sequences** the sweep's fragment pipeline had written into propsprites.js comments (an em-dash round-tripped through CP1252); comment-only, but that file is locked release surface. Pre-sweep baseline was clean. (2) **CENTRED SEAT** — the chair sat at the desk's left corner. Cause was NOT desk-specific: `PropAnchor.deriveAnchor` took the first walkable tile and `sideTiles` emits an edge west-to-east, so the approach tile for ANY multi-tile prop was its west-most one (bar/couch/bench too). `centreOut()` now orders an edge middle-first. But a whole tile is not the centre of an EVEN-width prop — a 2-wide desk spans 24px so either tile is 6px off, and moving left→right only swaps which side is wrong; so `deskSeat` also returns `cx`, the FRACTIONAL tile x centring a 1-wide seat, used by the chair render AND the seated-body foot snap (`seatFoot`), while the walk target stays a whole tile because pathing needs one. Chair and sitter derive from the same seat, so they cannot drift apart. Measured: 2-wide desk spans px 120..144 (centre 132), chair lands 126..138 — centre 132; 3/4/5-wide centre exactly. Andrew confirmed on a live dev-seed station. NOTE the wider blast radius: agents now approach the MIDDLE of any multi-tile prop, not its west corner. TRAP hit twice this lane: the claims gate is HEAD-sensitive and trunk moves under you — a gate run can go red purely because another session committed mid-run; re-run before diagnosing.

**2026-07-25 INCIDENT — a `git reset` on trunk silently deleted a merged lane, and the gate went GREEN over the hole.** Read this before the next merge. Sequence from `git reflog feat/harness-backend`: `@{7}` Tier-0 merged to trunk as `53bd090e` → `@{6}` another session committed a backdrop digest → `@{5}` **`reset: moving to 2bf21800`** → `@{4}` the digest re-committed → `@{3}` floor/wall merged → `@{1}` the honesty lane merged on top. The reset was almost certainly meant to undo only that session's own digest commit; it also discarded a merge that had landed in between, and every later merge was built on a trunk that had never contained Tier-0. **Net damage: `sidecar/providers/prices.js` (a SOURCE file), the truncated-stream fix in loop.js + all five providers, and 4 test files were absent from trunk while `test:fast` reported 387 steps GREEN** — the deleted tests took their own `fast.list` registrations with them, and a test that does not exist cannot fail. Caught only because the step count moved the WRONG WAY (387 where 389 was predicted: 386 base + 2 Tier-0 + 1 new). Recovered by re-merging the intact lane branch → `22679217`; all 4 tests registered and passing, `prices.js` restored, honesty lane preserved. **THREE LAWS: (1) `git reset` on trunk is never safe in a multi-session repo — `git revert` the specific commit instead; check `git log --oneline -3` first, another lane may have merged since you looked. (2) PREDICT the gate's step count before you run it and reconcile any difference — `lint-gate-can-fail` proves the REGISTERED steps can go red, it cannot prove the right steps are registered. The gate cannot detect its own amputation. (3) A green gate is evidence about the tree that was tested; after a concurrent merge or any history rewrite it is evidence about nothing.** Follow-up worth building: a guard asserting every `test/*.test.js` on disk appears in a list, so a vanished registration is loud.

**2026-07-25 merge digest (harness honesty — telling the model the truth about its own reach):** `claude/starnet-hermes-gaps-585d3a` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `5539c1dc`. Three lies aimed at the MODEL rather than the user, which is why no truth-audit ever caught them. **(1)** `capsummary` told the agent the WORKBENCH grants "control the desktop computer" — `computer.use`/`desktop.open` carry no capability grant at all, are stripped twice, and `desktop.open.run()` throws. **(2)** Capability presence was resolved by capId, but a capId OUTLIVES its headline tool: on any autonomous surface `enforceRunAuthority` strips `shell.exec`/`verify.run` while `workbench`'s background-shell and browser-test tools survive, so the block claimed shell was available when it was not. Presence now resolves against each capability's PROBE tool. **(3) Autonomous surfaces got NO capability block at all** — cron, night shift, workshop, delegated workers and every chat channel ran with no statement of their own powers, on exactly the surfaces where the authority gate had silently removed shell, live connector tools and Spotify. That is the mechanism behind the Telegram report where the agent demanded an OpenRouter key: it reached for a tool it did not have and rationalised the failure as a missing credential. They now get an honest block naming the unattended-run limit, told to report the blocker and explicitly forbidden from blaming credentials for an ungranted power. **Also fixed: the delegated-worker iteration cap was a DEAD WIRE** — `orchestration.js` always passed `maxIters`, but `runOnce` never read `o.maxIters` and the host never supplied `workerMaxIters`, so every worker ran the lead's full 40 instead of 10; guarded by a test asserting against a FAKE `runOnce`, permanently green over a disconnected seam. Now clamped downward only; a run passing no override is byte-identical. The `SAME ACCESS AS THE ORCHESTRATOR` comment described access the surface gate had already removed — corrected, not deleted, because the WORKBENCH grant workers receive is only PARTIALLY inert (background-shell and browser-test DO survive, so removing it would regress them). `capsummary.test.js` 33 assertions and **mutation-checked** (9 failures against the old code); new `worker-iteration-cap.test.js` (10 assertions) is a SOURCE-LOCK per the `harness-internal.test.js` convention, not a behavioural proof — it verifies both ends of the wire and the one-directional clamp, it does not drive a real worker. Analysis written up in `docs/HARNESS_GAP_2026-07-25.md` (de-identified per the reference-harness convention). Gate: **test:fast 389 + test:http green ON TRUNK**. Rides the next exe cut.

**2026-07-24 merge digest (command-surface verification sweep — LANE CLOSE):** `claude/starnet-command-improvements-753256` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `1199d4f6` (snapshot `7fe2bdf7`). Final pass over the whole command surface: all **43 builtins driven live** through the real `#chat-input` on :9400 (output captured, leak-to-model counted, state diffed before/after), plus a static cross-check of the three places every command is declared. **Silent commands** — a handler that returns without printing is indistinguishable from a broken app: `/stop` said NOTHING when nothing was running, `/retry` said nothing with no message to retry and nothing when busy; both now explain. (`/copy` checked and left alone — it reports via `StationUI.notify`, the established clipboard pattern.) `/retry`'s guards were split so each can speak; its "inert without an active idle stream" test was re-pinned BEHAVIOURALLY (both guards return before any `send`) instead of pinning the literal old expression. **/yolo** now states the TRANSITION (`ask first → full access`) — a bare `/yolo` TOGGLES, so the old wording read as a status report at the exact moment it had switched the approval gate off, the one setting where mistaking a change for a readout matters; an idempotent `/yolo off` reports "unchanged". **Registry/fallback drift**: every command is declared twice (sidecar/slash.js + chat.js `FALLBACK_SLASH_COMMANDS`, the latter being what the palette shows before the catalog lands or when the sidecar is down) and NOTHING compared them — 17 commands published an `argsHint` the fallback omitted entirely (user-visible now that argsHint renders: arg-taking commands looked arg-less on a cold start), `/routine`'s fallback hint was missing the `resume N` verb it implements, and `/steer`'s desc differed between the two with BOTH wrong about the handler (it steers a live run, queues only when it cannot reach one, refuses outright when idle). New **`test/slash.parity.test.js`** compares them field by field (names, aliases, desc, argsHint, action) and asserts no `dispatch:'server'` command has a local handler shadowing the sidecar — it PARSES the fallback array literal rather than grepping, so it cannot pass by accident. That guard's absence is why the drift happened; `slash.palette.test.js` only ever checked the strings appeared somewhere. Three registry hints corrected against their handlers (`/suggestions` demanded `accept N` while advertising bare `accept`; `/voice` accepts `handsfree` and never said so; `/new` `/clear` `/branch` all take a title and published no hint). **Atlas**: `/steer` still promised the pre-F3 behaviour (*"when idle it sends the note as the next turn"*) that `f488ed11` deleted for silently minting a paid run — and had been blessed **perfected AFTER** that deletion, so the audit itself was stale; dropped to mapped, promise corrected. 5 headlines refreshed against current descs, `goalloop.js`/`mintstore.js`/`index.js` added to three entries' wiring, `/reload-skills` no longer claims an `/api/skills` fetch the browser never makes. Area now **35 perfected + 8 mapped** — the 8 are honestly un-re-audited, NOT quietly re-blessed. Gate: **test:fast 379/379 + test:http green ON TRUNK**. Rides the next exe cut.

**2026-07-24 merge digest (/reasoning + /fast — dead doors onto a live dial):** `claude/starnet-command-improvements-753256` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `38c7d339` (snapshot `cbd7ef87`). **/reasoning** answered *"Reasoning effort is not a separate StarNet toggle yet"* to every argument — flatly false: `Harness.get/setReasoningEffort` scopes it per PROVIDER, the model dock sets it alongside model+provider, every `/api/run` payload carries it, and the sidecar `resolveReasoningEffort`s it and persists it on the roster. The dial was wired end to end; only the command never reached it. Now reads/writes the real vocabulary (`none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`) with three anti-fake guards: an unknown token is REFUSED (the harness normalizer maps anything unrecognized to `medium`, so `/reasoning turbo` would otherwise silently apply a level nobody asked for), the confirmation prints only after the store reads the value BACK, and a model with no reasoning dial gets a warn-not-block note (same precedent as `/model` vs the warmed catalog). **/fast** was a dead door (`argsHint:[status]`, every other arg → "not a separate runtime toggle yet"); grep confirms **no** fast-mode toggle exists anywhere — "fast" appears only as a model TIER tag in specialty resolution — so rather than keep a command that could only ever say no, it now drives the same real dial to `minimal` (which IS what makes replies quicker/cheaper), reports before→after, and no-ops honestly when already minimal. `applyAgentConfig` gained a `reasoningEffort` branch (it handled model/persona/approval but not effort, so no config seam existed to write through); it mirrors the model dock — harness store (what the next run reads) + the copy on the agent (what persists and reaches the roster). **/compress reviewed and deliberately NOT changed**: its `argsHint` is already empty, it promises only to "show context compaction status", and the auto-compaction it describes is REAL (`sidecar/loop.js` `maybeCompact` + a forced compact on the `context_overflow` recovery path) — an honest status readout, not a placeholder; the earlier lane report over-stated it. Live-proven at :9400 by hooking `fetch`: after `/reasoning xhigh` the captured `/api/run` body carried `reasoningEffort:"xhigh"`, after `/fast` the next carried `"minimal"`; `/reasoning turbo` refused without writing; survived a full page reload on both the harness store and the agent. **2026-07-24 merge digest (prop art — v4 material pass, all 116 props):** `claude/starnet-props-redesign-505089` -> `feat/harness-backend` merge `b7a7eeab` (snapshot `1189b648`), re-lock `qa(claims)` follow-up. Gate GREEN on trunk (378 steps). v3's projection/silhouette law is NOT reopened; v4 only fixes what it left flat — material and light. New shared kit above `const F`: `KEY/SKY`, `keyEdge`/`rimEdge` (warm ceiling key vs cool sky bounce — one temperature reads flat no matter how many ramp tones a prop has), `bloom` (3-ring falloff replacing `glow`'s single flat alpha rect), `spill`, `chamf` (k-px corners where `rr` cut 1), `cable`, `knurl`, `dial`, `codeRow`. `studio` and `missionboard` predated the style law and were rebuilt to it. Footprints, collision and every live-state contract unchanged; several props' live state became legible for the first time (`connector_portal` now separates its four states on aperture shape / crown motion / lamp cadence / conduit traffic, because the lightmap flattens hue alone). **Three bugs fixed that were already shipping:** `wear()` did `hx >> 5` on a `U.hash` UINT32 — the signed shift goes negative above 2^31 and JS `%` keeps the sign, so ~2/3 of scuff specks landed ABOVE the prop; present since the v7 port, affecting every prop that calls it (fixed to `>>>` here and in world.js's `fwear` copy); `treasury_coinsorter` passed `U.shade(c, -10)` against a -1..1 contract; `pub_outboundchute` drew a 12px text stencil on a 12px prop. **One regression caught in-lane:** briefing agents "no vertical rise off the deck" lifted 7 props out of their own footprint tile (`starchart` 100% -> 6% of art inside its tile), which would render them offset from the click with collision mismatched — now a standing gate, all 116 anchored. Proofs: 116 props x 16 state/time combos with no throws / no NaN / balanced ctx / in-range `U.shade`; walked-over props (rug, cablerun, hazardpad, graffiti) flat in the ground plane; `npm run golden` moved NOTHING (`ingame` diff=0.10) — the pass is layout-neutral. **Not mine, still open:** `build-skills` golden CHANGED diff=2.36 (pre-existing cut blocker, re-bless from the pin) and the war-room family's `#ff4a3d` room identity IS `ACC.alert`, so a real failure cannot signal distinctly there — a v2 lock, Andrew's call.

**2026-07-24 merge digest (/usage + /tools honesty):** `claude/starnet-command-improvements-753256` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `1189b648` (snapshot `4ca9096c`). Two truthful-telemetry violations found during the command-doors lane, fixed on its `dispatch:'server'` seam. **/usage** read `Harness.totals()` — a counter that only accumulates runs THIS BROWSER watched — and labelled it "lifetime": every routine, away shift, night-shift beat and Telegram run spends real money it never saw, so the headline figure structurally under-reported and disagreed with the ledger the budget caps enforce against. It now reads that ledger (today vs daily cap · all-time + runs + tokens · this agent's share), renders sub-cent spend as `<$0.01` not `$0.00`, and REFUSES on an unreadable ledger rather than reporting zero. **/tools** printed a hardcoded literal (`toolRows`) keyed only on placed props, wrong twice: the names had drifted badly from CAP_REGISTRY (`web` claimed web_search+web_fetch where the registry grants **14** tools incl. the whole browser suite; `memory` claimed 2 where it grants 10) **and** it ignored the TOOLSETS switch entirely, so a family the user had switched OFF still read as available though no run would ever be handed those tools. It now serves the same rows the TOOLSETS console renders, split active / placed-but-switched-OFF / locked-until-placed, and calls `compute` a grant rather than a callable tool. Browser-side readouts DELETED, not kept as fallbacks — falling back to them would reintroduce the lie on any sidecar hiccup; `placed` now rides the dispatch ctx since only the browser knows the live floor. Live-proven at :9400: ledger seeded with 3 headless runs ($1.504) → `/usage` reported $1.50 / 3 runs / 43,900 tokens with per-agent $0.42 vs $1.08, matching `GET /api/budget/status`; toggling the cabinet toolset OFF moved FILE CABINET to "switched OFF" and dropped the active count 4→3. Noted-not-fixed: `GET /api/budget/status`'s `caps` key is clobbered by `budget.status()`'s pool-only shape (pre-existing; the Budget panel already compensates with its own precedence chain, and `/usage` reads `effectiveCaps` directly). Atlas: both entries dropped **perfected → mapped** (implementation changed, prior audit stale) — area now 38 perfected + 5 mapped. Gate: **test:fast 378/378 + test:http green ON TRUNK**. Rides the next exe cut.

**2026-07-24 merge digest (command doors — server-executed slash commands):** `claude/starnet-command-improvements-753256` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `0e91fc69` (snapshot `716f68bb`). The slash layer had 40 commands against 180 sidecar routes and only ~12 touched the backend, because `slash.dispatch()` could only return `client`/`insert` directives — a command reached a route only via a hand-written `fetch()` in chat.js (which is how `/build-away` drifted into existing ONLY in chat.js's fallback list, invisible to `/api/slash/catalog` and to the Atlas). Adds `dispatch:'server'` + `sidecar/slash-actions.js`: the sidecar executes the command and returns a `{type:'say'}` directive the browser only prints, so a later channel-hub/`/v1` surface can run the same command and read the same answer. Ships **/routine** (cron CRUD; create+preview share the extracted `createCronJobFromSpec`/`cronPreviewOf` with the HTTP routes so the command and the ROUTINES panel cannot disagree about when a routine fires — no run-now verb, that streams NDJSON and stays in the panel), **/away** (the 14-route away-workshop subsystem, subsuming `/build-away` as an alias), **/loop** (in-session interval watcher; capped 20 runs, ≥1min cadence, in-memory by design). Also fixed in the same seam: `argsHint` was published by the registry but dropped in chat.js so NO command ever showed it took an argument, and a server command with no local action hit a null `run()` inside a bare catch (silent no-op when the sidecar was unreachable). An adversarial review pass then caught two real lies that are now fixed + tested: `/away now` reported a FAILED shift (`fired:true`, reason `run-failed`/`no-manifest`) as "finished work waits in OUTBOX", and a `/loop` tick could ANSWER the station's own awakening interview because `send()` routes text into `interview()`/`TaskIntent` — `loopTick` now obeys `goalBlocked()` like the goal loop. Live-proven at :9400: routine created→persisted across a real sidecar restart→paused→deleted with independent `GET /api/cron` proof; cross-stream canary showed 0 contamination over 2 tick intervals; a blocking Dialogue held the loop at 0 fires then it resumed and fired twice on release; routine names containing `<img onerror>` render as literal text (0 injected elements). W0 claims surface re-stamped in-branch twice (chat.js is pinned; the audit reads the ledger from the HEAD blob, so the re-lock must be COMMITTED). Gate: **test:fast 378/378 green + test:http green ON TRUNK**. Atlas: 3 new command entries at `mapped` (40 perfected + 3 mapped = 43) — deliberately NOT claimed perfected, no Perfectionist live-audit pass was run. Rides the next exe cut.

**2026-07-22 merge digest (desktop close save-loss fix — the v0.6.6 driver):** `claude/starnet-data-loss-close-a55e80` → `feat/harness-backend` fast-forward `bac344eb`+`5a3bdbce` (snapshot `261eb2ce`). Member-reported v0.6.5 data loss on app close, root-caused to THREE stacked bugs in the save write-through: (1) installUnloadFlush's relative sendBeacon posted into the tauri.localhost bundled-asset protocol (only window.fetch is rewritten to the sidecar loopback URL) and vanished; (2) sendBeacon cannot carry X-StarNet-Token so it would have 403'd anyway; (3) sendBeacon's `true` (= queued for dispatch) was treated as a confirmed 200 — `pending` nulled + health stamped OK, so the newest save was DESTROYED client-side on every desktop close AND minimize/hide-to-tray while the save-dot claimed "backed up". Fix: beacon aims at the absolute `window.__STARNET_API__` endpoint with the per-launch `?token=` credential as a text/plain blob (CORS-simple), dispatch is NEVER success (pending survives; the hide handler also force-flushes via the confirmable fetch, the only path allowed to stamp health); apiauth gains the pure tested `queryTokenRoute` matrix (GET/HEAD /api/file + POST /api/save only); the POST /api/save route becomes `qsplit` (the `exact:` matcher compares the FULL url incl. query — the beacon's ?token= 404'd before auth; TRAP generalizes). Live-proven on a booted sidecar: header-less query-token text/plain POST lands on disk ok:true; wrong/missing token 403; query token opens NO other route; anti-clobber still refuses stale writes. New `test/cloudsave-unload.test.js` (16 asserts) locks the contract. Gates: trunk **test:fast 377/377** + **test:http 4/4 suites green** at `5a3bdbce`; W0 re-stamped in-branch at `bac344eb`. No shared/events.js change; no credential moves. Member recovery path: `%APPDATA%\ai.skynet.harness\workspaces\agent.save.json` (+.bak). STILL OPEN: rolling save-envelope restore points under SYSTEM (RESTORE POINTS only cover workbench files). Commit folds the Guardian dashboard row refresh (GREEN 22:05Z @ 261eb2ce) sitting uncommitted in the integration tree.

**2026-07-19 merge digest (text-size laptop lane):** `claude/starnet-text-size-laptop-e44a8d` → `feat/harness-backend` merge `6e778372` (snapshot `6825646e`) + fresh W0 re-stamp `8338eefd` (claims 37/181 planning PASS on trunk). Andrew's report: text extremely small on a laptop, COMMS worst. Root cause: ~800 hard-px font-size declarations (COMMS 10–13.5px) that no font token reaches, and no screen-size adaptation. Shipped: (1) **TEXT SIZE defaults to AUTO** — resolved from the screen's long edge in CSS px (≤1470→115%, ≤1740→110%, else 100%) so laptop installs boot readable with nothing to find; chip row AUTO/COMPACT 90/STANDARD 100/LARGE 115/X-LARGE 130/HUGE 145 in SETTINGS→APPEARANCE→DISPLAY (`settings.textScale`, 0=auto; AUTO chip title states the resolved %; re-resolves on monitor change). Mechanism = body zoom; world.js+build.js resize() bake the zoom into the canvas backing store so the pixel-art stage/REFIT floor stay 1:1 device pixels. (2) **Coordinate-space law** — style px are body-zoomed space, clientX/rects/innerWidth are visual px; new `U.uiZoom()` converts at every mixer: stationui window drag/resize/cascade/clamp + zoom-change re-clamp + oversize-window shrink in fitTermInViewport, COMMS seam (chatresize), ws/project context menus + model pop (app.js), hint bubble, tutorial coach/brief, widget popover. ANY future floating UI mixing those spaces must divide by uiZoom(). Live-proven on seed :9231 at 1366×768: AUTO→115 with nothing stored; zero page/topbar/bottombar/COMMS overflow at 115 AND 145; settings console fits at 145 after the zoom-change re-clamp (12,12→1354,756); window drag tracks the cursor exactly with honest edge clamps; seam width math exact under 1.45; stage bitmap == rect×dpr (ratio 1.0). **2026-07-14 merge digest (update-blockers lane, part 6 — update "no matter what" + mac test path):** lane → trunk FF `1884393f`. TWO deliverables for "mac must update + updates must never break setups": (1) GUARANTEED MANUAL FALLBACK — the Update Center now always offers a manual path (DOWNLOAD LATEST MANUALLY on any error + a persistent `download manually` footer link → releases page via `open_external_url`, `window.open` fallback), so a blocked auto-update (Gatekeeper on unsigned mac, network/perm/disk) never strands a user; reinstalling over the top keeps ALL data. (2) DATA-PRESERVATION verified in code + documented: user state lives OUTSIDE the app bundle the installer replaces — workspaces in Application Support, localStorage/IndexedDB in the WebView store keyed by the UNCHANGED bundle id, and the version-change purge only drops regenerable GPU/compiled caches (explicitly excludes Local Storage/IndexedDB/Cookies, `main.rs:509`) and is a no-op on mac. So an update physically cannot touch a user's station, on any platform. (3) `docs/MAC_UPDATE_TEST.md` — exact real-Mac proof runbook (real-endpoint path, unadvertised repo = audience of one; NOT prerelease — `releases/latest` skips prereleases; seed recognisable state → update → verify mechanism AND survival). Gate 322/322, W0 PASS. HONEST: mac auto-update still needs the one real Mac run (runbook ready, tester lined up); the manual fallback + data-preservation guarantee mean even a mac auto-update failure is recoverable without data loss.

**2026-07-14 merge digest (update-blockers lane, part 5 — macOS update-restart guard):** lane → trunk FF `be8d3470`. Code-audit of the plugin's per-platform install paths found a mac/linux hazard we introduced this session: mac/linux end an install with `app.restart()` (Windows `process::exit()`s in the plugin BEFORE reaching the command's restart, so it never applies) — if restart emits a window close-requested event, the GB-4 quit guard would see live agents + `preventDefault()`, swapping the update files but never relaunching (stuck update); the "INSTALL ANYWAY with agents running" path makes it reachable. FIX: `Updates.isInstalling()` flips true once the install invoke fires; `quitguard.js` allows any close while an update installs (drains state first). Defensive + cross-platform (no-op on Windows, essential on mac). Live-verified in dev seed: update-restart close w/ live agents passes + no card; normal live-agent close still blocks. +4 quitguard assertions (22). Gate 320/320, W0 PASS. HONEST SCOPE: this removes one code-findable mac hazard from a Windows box — the mac update path (`.app.tar.gz` bundle swap, Unix-safe, no lock-hang possible) is architecturally sound but has ZERO end-to-end proof; a real Mac canary run is still required and is blocked on Mac hardware (the canary is Windows-only + CDP-drive doesn't port to WKWebView).

**2026-07-14 merge digest (update-blockers lane, part 4 — CANARY CAUGHT A P0):** `claude/starnet-update-blockers-d0a65b` → `feat/harness-backend` fast-forward → `72dea45a`. The local update canary (part 3) was RUN end-to-end on real signed builds and immediately caught a UNIVERSAL Windows in-app-update hang no suite test could see: the updater plugin launches NSIS then hard-exits via `std::process::exit(0)`, which does NOT fire Tauri's `ExitRequested` handler where `kill_sidecar()` runs — so the old `node.exe` sidecar stays alive holding a write lock and NSIS FREEZES on "error opening file for writing: node.exe" forever (every Windows user, every update). FIX (`src-tauri/src/main.rs` `starnet_update_check`): wire the plugin's `on_before_exit` hook to set `shutting_down` + `kill_sidecar()` before the exit; the hook rides the pending Update into the install path. PROVEN both directions on real signed 0.5.0/0.5.1 canary builds: pre-fix hung on the lock dialog (recovered manually), post-fix 0.5.0→0.5.1 CLEAN — version advanced, installer EXITED, app relaunched itself; independently confirmed, real StarNet install untouched throughout. Also hardened `update-canary.mjs drive()`: a clean update now requires installer-exited AND app-relaunched, not just the version resource — NSIS flips the exe version BEFORE hitting the locked node.exe, so version-only was a FALSE GREEN (the first receipt was wrong for exactly this reason; now a hung installer past deadline reports the sidecar diagnostic). Gate: `test:fast` 320/320 at the FF'd commit; W0 planning PASS. This is the canary earning its entire cost on first run.

## Green Guardian (lane Q1)

One cycle = pin trunk into a **dedicated** checkout and run the four detectors against it:
`test:fast` → `shoot` → `golden` → `audit`. One deduped ledger finding per regression
(fingerprinted per failing suite/frame/assertion so the same defect never re-nags), the
row above refreshed, nonzero exit on any red. Run it:

```
npm run qa:guardian            # one cycle; exits nonzero if trunk is red/blocked
npm run qa:guardian -- --skip-visual   # test:fast + audit only (no Chrome; CI-lite)
npm run qa:guardian:watch      # poll trunk HEAD; run a cycle when it moves
```

**Pinned checkout (read-only law).** Gates NEVER run in the integration tree or another
agent's worktree. The Guardian owns a detached `git worktree` at `../_qa-guardian-pin`
(override with `SKYNET_GUARDIAN_PIN`), `git reset --hard`'d to the current trunk head each
cycle (created + `npm install`'d on first run). Sidecar/CDP ports stay in the Guardian
range: shoot `8940/9340`, golden `8941/9341`, audit `8942/9342`.

**STATUS.md + findings target the guardian's OWN repo, not the pin.** The row above and
`qa/findings/*.json` are written into the qa/ dir of the repo the guardian *script* lives
in (resolved from `import.meta.url`), so the dashboard reflects live state and survives the
pinned checkout's next `reset --hard`. Evidence (logs, flagged golden PNGs, gate reports)
is copied into `.bugloops/guardian-<stamp>/` for the same reason. Findings are filed
through `scripts/qa/ledger.mjs --add` so dedup / known-refusal stays the ONE implementation.

**No-fake-green.** A step that cannot run (git/npm/spawn failure, timeout, missing report)
files a **P0 BLOCKED** finding loudly and the cycle exits nonzero — it never silently
passes. Scheduling (Task Scheduler vs. a `/loop` session) is lane Q5's job; this script is
schedule-agnostic.

**Dismissed-frame gate (golden).** A golden frame whose *current* fingerprint is already
**dismissed/known** in the ledger is **review-clean**, not a regression: `scripts/golden.mjs`
asks the ledger's `suppressedFingerprints()` (the ONE dedup/known authority) and computes each
frame's Green-Guardian fingerprint (`goldenFrameFingerprint(name)` == `fingerprintOf({crew:'Green
Guardian', checkId:'golden', subject:'frame/'+name})`). A match is logged as `review-clean …
matches dismissed finding <fp>` and kept OUT of `flagged`, so golden exits 0 and the row above
stays GREEN. This exists because `sys-rewind` (the one modal that doesn't full-bleed over the
animated CRT floor) diffs forever as animation noise — its finding `01c40465` was triaged and
**dismissed**, yet a naïve golden gate would re-flag it every cycle and pin this row RED with 0
open findings (a lying dashboard). **Narrow by design:** only a frame whose fingerprint is on the
dismissed/known baseline is excused; a new frame, a different frame, or a diff on a non-dismissed
frame STILL flags → exit 3 → the Guardian files it through the ledger exactly as before. Fail-open:
if the ledger can't be read, nothing is suppressed. Covered by `test/golden.test.js`.

## Ledger quick reference

```
# file a finding (rejected without evidence; refused if known/dismissed; deduped by fingerprint)
node scripts/qa/ledger.mjs --add --json '{"crew":"Green Guardian","severity":"P0","title":"...","detail":"...","evidence":["path/to/artifact"],"checkId":"...","subject":"..."}'

# morning report (grouped by severity then crew); --write persists to qa/digests/<date>.md
node scripts/qa/ledger.mjs --digest [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--write]

# is this fingerprint already filed / known?
node scripts/qa/ledger.mjs --dedup-check <fingerprint>

# per-crew roll-up (the table above, live)
node scripts/qa/ledger.mjs --status
```

- 2026-07-15 scheduler-reliability: claude/starnet-scheduler-audit-1b33c2 -> trunk 5dcd3868: FOUR of the six 2026-07-15 scheduler-audit gaps closed in sidecar cron. (1) MISFIRE POLICY — a recurring fire past its grace window now consults job.misfire ('fire_once' collapses the missed backlog into ONE catch-up run, default for cron schedules + intervals >=1h; 'skip' = old behavior, default for fast intervals) so opening StarNet at 12:01 no longer silently loses the 9:00 routine; additive/editable field, normalized in the store. (2) TRANSACTIONAL DISPATCH — a launch is conditional on a verified durable advance/claim: index.js setJobs returns an honest receipt (false = write never hit disk, RAM mirror rolled back to disk state so jobs stay due); the driver fires NOTHING over a failed receipt (all planned fires defer, retry next tick). (3) GENERATION FENCE — only the run still owning its lease may markRun; a zombie-swept run settling late can no longer overwrite the replacement's record (emits cron.result tagged 'stale-lease' instead). (4) TICKER HEALTH + DELIVERY OUTCOMES — GET /api/cron carries additive health {lastTickAt,lastSuccessAt,lastTickError,healthy = armed AND clean tick within 3 periods}; autonotify send attempts report per-send outcomes, {ok:false} SendResults normalized to real failures, persisted as lastDeliveryAt/Ok/Error via the new markDelivery reducer. New test/cron.dispatch.test.js (21 assertions, fast gate) locks fail-then-recover exactly-once + replacement-record survival + fenced abort. Live-proven on a real booted sidecar (isolated ws, zero spend): health real+healthy over HTTP, 3h-stale daily PLANNED AS FIRE (no-capability at the gate, no caught-up skip), nextRunAt fast-forwarded, legacy jobs load fine. - 2026-07-16 claude/starnet-task-board-popup-0403c1 -> trunk 87a7ffdb (merge): NAVDOCK FOCUS FIX — the TASK BOARD no longer pops open on stray keypresses. Root cause: navdock.js moved keyboard focus onto the WORK dock popover's FIRST item (TASKS) on EVERY open including mouse opens, so any later stray Enter/Space "clicked" TASKS; the trigger also kept focus, so Space→dock reopens→Space→board opened with no click near it (always the task board = always the first WORK item). (2) brief_ask `recommended` had to byte-match an option, so a trailing period silently cost the ★ chip and looked identical to "the agent had no opinion" (hidden tool => unlogged); now tier-1/2 normalized matching plus a warn line so the rejection rate is knowable. - 2026-07-25: API-CONNECTION GAP — three stacked lanes (`claude/api-connection-p0` → `claude/web-request-tool` → `claude/connect-platform-catalog`) -> trunk merge `586e7acb` (snapshot `39afdf06`), re-locks `481eab54` / `87c9f9ea`. From a user report that Printify would not connect; NOTHING shipped is Printify-specific — other harnesses have no Printify-specific code either, they just have a generic path that works, and that is what was missing. **(1) KEYS-tab keys NEVER reached an agent's shell:** `servicekeys.deriveEnvVar` always ends `_API_KEY` and `environment.sanitizeChildEnv` strips every `*_KEY`/`*_TOKEN`, so 100% of pasted keys were scrubbed from the child env while `promptBlock` still told the model "available to your shell commands ... call that service's API directly (curl etc.)" — the model expanded an empty string and got a 401. Broke EVERY platform, not just POD. Fixed with a per-call `serviceEnv` hook (`servicekeys.runEnv`), deliberately NOT a boot snapshot: a key pasted after boot is live on the very next run, a disabled one gone on the very next run. Provider/billing keys stay reserved and still never reach a child. The comment that caused it claimed shell children "inherit" `process.env` — they do not; corrected in place. **(2) The MCP STDIO tab could never connect:** `makeStdioTransport` requires `deps.userControlIsolated === true`, which appears in exactly THREE places repo-wide (the check plus two lines in `test/mcp.stdio.test.js`) and is set NOWHERE in production; the installed app also pins `STARNET_MCP_STDIO=0` (`src-tauri/src/main.rs`, locked by `test/tauri.hardening.test.js`). The pane offered command/args/cwd/env and advertised seven launchers anyway, saved the config ENABLED, and the doomed row was retried every boot forever. The backend was always right — the frontend lied. The tab now explains why and points at the two routes that work, with ADD disabled; legacy rows say the same. `test/connectors-ui.test.js` had been PINNING the lie in place (asserting the inputs must exist) — inverted. **(3) NEW `web_request(url, method, headers, body)`** — the first tool in StarNet that can send a custom HTTP header (`web_fetch`'s entire schema is `{url}`), so an authenticated API no longer requires handing the agent a full shell. THE KEY NEVER ENTERS MODEL CONTEXT: the model writes a `${NAME}` placeholder and the host substitutes at send time; placeholders resolve in HEADERS ONLY, because a secret in a URL leaks into history, proxy logs and Referer. Rides a placed **dish**, not a workbench. Guards: existing SSRF guard, per-hop redirect re-validation, credentials DROPPED on a cross-host redirect, CRLF header-injection refusal, response cap, redaction, response fenced as untrusted. Unattended use is granted PER PLATFORM KEY (default OFF, `POST /api/servicekeys/autonomy`, toggle in the KEYS tab) via a consent tier modelled on the Away Workshop grant. **PROVEN FIRST, LIVE, that the gap was real:** a real sidecar with an MCP connector reporting `up` with 1 tool offered `mcp__probe__ping` + `shell_exec` on an interactive run and **NEITHER** on a cron run — connectors are account-level and ride both surfaces, so the surface gate in `inputpolicy.js` was the only possible cause. Unattended agents had ZERO authenticated-platform reach while still being handed `browser_login`. **(4) A curated PLATFORM directory in KEYS + `website/docs/connect-a-platform.html`.** Probed before building: there is NO remote MCP server for printify, printful, etsy or woocommerce (all 404) and `mcp.shopify.dev` does not even resolve; Shopify's official Storefront MCP is per-store and the catalog has a fixed `url` field with no template support. So seeding commerce catalog rows would have been a lie — the generic route was the answer and only needed to be findable. **TRAPS:** `scope:'execute'` in this codebase means *run a host process* and hits the autonomous exec lockout in `permissions.js` BEFORE any per-key grant is consulted — a network write is `scope:'write'`, which is ALSO what keeps `web_request` permanently under that lockout (no shell/spawn/filesystem is reachable from it on any surface). A new tool must be registered in FIVE places or a gate goes red: CAP_REGISTRY, `frontend/app/toolprops.js` (object=capability law), the exact-array pin in `test/harness.integration.test.js`, plus the `chat.js`/`stationui.js` listings. `test/servicekeys.test.js` was ORPHANED — passing, but referenced by NO gate (adopted into fast.list). **SIX fast-gate tests use `_assert.js` but end in a bare `console.log` instead of `A.report()`, so they print FAIL and still exit 0** — `diagnostics-support-email`, `interests`, `scout`, `run-truth`, `mcp.catalog`, `widgets` (chip filed, deliberately NOT fixed here: each may go genuinely red once the exit works and needs real triage). A draft of the platform directory contained an OpenRouter row whose derived env var is RESERVED, so the card would have sat in the picker and always failed on save — the test now runs a REAL upsert per row so no dead-click row can be added. Merge conflicts: `chat.js` (trunk DELETED the browser-side `toolRows()` in favour of `dispatch:'server'` — took trunk's deletion; CAP_REGISTRY already carries the `web_request` dish grant, so the server-side `/tools` reports it and there is no second list left to drift) and `claims.json` (releaseSurface only — trunk changed 0 claims, this lane changed 1, zero overlap). `npm run sync:website` regenerated the app mirror and caught `stationui.js` + `toolprops.js`, which the hand-mirror had MISSED and which would have shipped the web build out of sync — website/app is GENERATED, never hand-edit it. Gate: **test:fast 384/384 + FULL test:http green ON TRUNK**; live-verified post-merge (stdio pane 0 inputs / ADD disabled, HTTP tab intact, 10 platform cards across 5 categories, key round-trip masked `····7788` + unattended toggle both directions, 0 console errors). **NOT PUSHED** — this merge touches `website/` and `deploy-website.yml` fires on push to `feat/harness-backend` for those paths, so pushing auto-deploys starnetos.com; that is Andrew's call, not part of the merge authorization. OPEN: query-param API keys unsupported (`web_request` refuses a secret in a URL by design — chip filed for a host-side append mode); the docker exec backend passes no env at all, so service keys do not reach a containerised shell (chip filed); the Green Guardian dashboard row committed alongside this digest stamped trunk GREEN at `39afdf06`, the PRE-merge head — it needs a re-run before it can be read as current.
- 2026-07-25: BROWSER CDP-TIMEOUT WEDGE + GATE-HONESTY HARDENING — `claude/browser-cdp-timeouts` -> trunk merge (snapshot `62706fb9`), re-lock `9ccb8119`. Closes the last thread from the user transcript: `browser_navigate` CDP-timed-out and then `browser_snapshot` CDP-timed-out too. **REPRODUCED** against the REAL `makeCdpDriver` with an injected CDP socket whose `Page.navigate` never answers. Mechanism: **Page.navigate does not resolve until the navigation COMMITS**, so a slow origin or an anti-bot challenge blew the 15s command budget sized for `Runtime.evaluate`; nothing then cancelled the load, so the renderer stayed busy and the NEXT call queued behind it and timed out too — **one stalled page took out the entire session**, which reads as "the browser is broken". Probe confirmed `stopLoading attempted: false`. FIX: navigation gets its OWN budget (~30s) via a new optional per-call timeout on `CdpClient.send`; on timeout we ALWAYS `Page.stopLoading` so the very next call works; the error names the host, says the load was stopped, and points at `browser.get_text` or the site's API via `web_request`. **TRAP THAT NEARLY SHIPPED WRONG:** `browser.navigate` had a **20s TOOL budget** (from the shared `read()` wrapper), so a 30s driver budget would NEVER be reached — the outer `withTimeout` in registry.dispatch would abort `run()` mid-flight and the stopLoading recovery would never execute, leaving exactly the wedge. Tool budget raised to 45s and **a test pins the ordering because the two numbers live in different places**. Both guarantees mutation-checked. **GATE HONESTY, round 2:** writing that test exposed that `report()` being PRESENT is not the same as `report()` being REACHED — my new browser test exited mid-await with code 0 (the CDP client **unrefs** its timeout timers, so the event loop drained) and scored GREEN having verified nothing. `test/_assert.js` now installs a process-exit guard: any file that ran assertions and exits 0 without report() is turned RED with the cause. That guard IMMEDIATELY caught a pre-existing case the static lint had passed for months — `continuation-guard.test.js` settled only via `.catch(... process.exit(1))`, which fires on a THROWN error, so all 16 of its assertions could fail and it still exited 0. Tightening `lint-gate-can-fail` to stop accepting a bare `process.exit(` as evidence found **six more** in the same shape (text-toolcall-markup, model-conformance, titlebar, mcp.oauth, widgetfeed, scout.http). All fixed, all still pass, **399 asserting gate steps now verified able to fail**. FLAKE FOUND (not mine, chipped): `test/channels.telegram.buttons.e2e.test.js` fails "double-tap never starts a second run — expected 2, got 4" under full-suite load but passes 3/3 alone — if the race is in the PRODUCT rather than the test, a slow moment could start duplicate runs from one tap, which costs the user money. Gate: **test:fast 392 + full test:http green ON TRUNK**.
- 2026-07-25: WEB_FETCH JINA IS DEAD KEYLESS — `claude/webfetch-jina-dead` -> trunk merge `b30d4b3c` (snapshot `dd627971`), re-lock `923a86a8`. Traced from a REAL USER TRANSCRIPT ("sometimes my agent finds some way around, other times not"), which showed an Etsy/Printify agent hitting three separate failures: an unspecified tool failure, browser CDP timeouts, and `web_fetch` returning HTTP 403. Reproduced the 403, then isolated WHO was blocking: **Jina Reader now returns 401 AuthenticationRequiredError for EVERY url without a token** — verified against example.com, Wikipedia AND a vendor docs site, so it is auth, not a rate limit; its keyless tier is gone. The code still described it as "PRIMARY: Jina Reader. Keyless 20 RPM" and attempted it FIRST on every single `web_fetch`, so **every fetch in every install paid a guaranteed-failing round-trip** and then silently fell back to `directFetch` — our own cruder `htmlToText`, and refused outright by anti-bot sites. Fetch quality had degraded product-wide with nothing saying so. FIX: Jina is attempted ONLY when a key is configured; without one we go straight to direct (one hop, not two), with one the good extraction returns. `deps.jinaKey` is finally WIRED (it existed in the code but was never passed by index.js), resolved from the KEYS store through the same per-key unattended grant, so an ungranted autonomous run degrades to direct rather than failing. Jina added to the platform directory as an explicitly OPTIONAL row. **TRAP:** `test/harness.integration.test.js` broke, and the reason IS the finding — its canned fetch answered ONLY `r.jina.ai`, so the integration mission had been exercising a path no default install ever takes; it now serves the article on the direct url. NOT fixed deliberately: Etsy 403s direct fetches regardless of User-Agent (verified with a browsery UA too) — that is their anti-bot working, and dressing requests up to evade it is not something StarNet should do; the honest Etsy route is its API via `web_request`. STILL OPEN from the same transcript: the browser CDP timeouts (15s default, `browser.js:286`) belong to the browser-parity lane, and the unspecified "tool failure" was never identified. Gate: **test:fast 391 + full test:http green ON TRUNK**.

Closes the three chips left open by the API-connection merge. **(1) SEVEN gate steps could NEVER turn the gate red.** `test/_assert.js` accumulates failures in a counter and ONLY `report()` calls `process.exit(fail?1:0)`, so a file ending in a bare `console.log('... OK')` printed `FAIL: ...` for every broken assertion and STILL exited 0 — the runner scored it green. Affected: `diagnostics-support-email`, `interests`, `scout`, `run-truth`, `mcp.catalog`, `widgets`, and `servicekeys` (which the PREVIOUS lane adopted INTO the gate while broken — the fix introduced a seventh instance of the bug it was documenting). Checked first: **none were actually failing**, so nothing had been concealed; the defect is that none of them COULD have caught a future regression. Each now ends in `A.report()` and each was MUTATION-CHECKED (inject a failing assertion, confirm exit 1). **New `test/lint-gate-can-fail.js` makes the hole unreopenable** — it enumerates the REAL gate (`test/fast.list` + the `test:http:raw` chain parsed out of package.json), so a step added to either gate is covered automatically with no second list to drift; 393 asserting steps verified. **Writing that lint caught a bug in the lint itself:** the explanatory comment added above each fixed test contains the words `report()` and `process.exit(`, so a naive scan matched the DOCUMENTATION and happily passed a file whose real code had been reverted to a bare console.log — comments are now stripped before the check, proven by re-breaking a file and watching the lint go red. A lint that reads its own docs as proof is the same class of bug it exists to catch. **(2) The docker exec backend passed NO environment into the container**, so KEYS-tab service keys silently did not exist for a containerised shell (`STARNET_EXEC_BACKEND=docker`). Fixed with `docker run -e NAME` with **no** `=value`: docker forwards that variable from its OWN client environment, so the argv carries only the NAME (safe in any `ps` listing) and the value never touches a command line or a temp file on disk — deliberately NOT `-e NAME=value` and NOT `--env-file`. Same per-call contract as local (revoke takes effect on the next run), with reserved provider keys and `STARNET_`/`SKYNET_` safety pins filtered out and env names validated so a malformed one cannot become a docker flag. Mutation-checked against a leak onto argv. **(3) `web_request` gains an AUTH DESCRIPTOR** for the real class of APIs that will not accept a header: `auth: {"key":"SOME_API_KEY","in":"query","name":"api_key"}` (`in:"header"` with optional `prefix` also supported). The rule is unchanged — the model NAMES the key and the slot, the HOST attaches the value after the request is composed, so no secret ever appears in a URL the model wrote; attached to the FIRST HOP ONLY so an open redirect cannot carry it onward, and the response label already excluded the query string. `auth.key` must match a NAME shape (`^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*$`), refusing a literal value the model might otherwise smuggle in, and the per-key unattended grant governs this path through the same resolver. Both guards mutation-checked. Docs updated. **TRAPS:** locking `releaseSurface` to HEAD and testing WITHOUT committing fails — the claims gate asserts `candidateCommit !== sourceCommit`, so the re-lock must be its own commit (hit again this lane). Trunk moved 6 commits mid-lane (a Telegram-buttons lane landed); the merge was clean but the post-merge live smoke was therefore run against TRUNK, not the lane, and confirmed both surfaces coexist (connectors + the messaging console's 5 rail dots, 0 console errors). Gate: **test:fast 386/386 + FULL test:http green ON TRUNK**, lint reports 393 asserting steps all able to fail. **STILL NOT PUSHED** — trunk touches `website/` and `deploy-website.yml` fires on push for those paths, so pushing auto-deploys starnetos.com; that remains Andrew's call. Also still owed: a Green Guardian re-run (its dashboard row stamped `39afdf06`, several merges back).
- 2026-07-25: TELEGRAM INLINE KEYBOARDS — branch `claude/starnet-telegram-improvements-7fb658` (1c934ec7 + 9980aff4 + 522d5c0e) -> trunk merge `869a8c25` (snapshot `44285592`). From a user report that the Telegram bridge is bare-bones: a multiple-choice question rendered as a numbered list to retype, and anything needing permission was silently skipped because a headless run has no way to ask. Mined the reference harness for the pattern, then built the smallest honest version against our seams. **THE INBOUND HALF WAS ALREADY BUILT AND UNIT-TESTED** — `callback_query` was parsed in `telegram.js`, owner-gated in `adapter.js`, and routed to `hub.onCallback`, whose entire body was the comment `/* C6: route ... */`; and `send(chatId,text,opts)` already splats opts into the sendMessage payload, so `reply_markup` flowed end to end with nothing ever passing it. The real new code is one module. **(1) NEW `channels/prompts.js`** — the bounded token→meaning registry + `callback_data` codec. Telegram caps `callback_data` at 64 BYTES, so the button carries only `<kind>:<token>:<idx>` (~13 bytes) and the payload stays server-side; a 4000-char option produces the same tiny button. Entries are single-use, bounded at 200 oldest-first, and resolved BY TOKEN. **(2) MULTIPLE CHOICE** — a `TASK_QUESTION` reply ships an inline keyboard; the FULL option text stays in the body (Telegram truncates long button labels on a phone) and the button carries a short numbered echo. A tap re-enters through `processInbound` carrying the option's OWN text, so it is byte-identical to the Commander typing it — history, routing, Task Brief continuity and the run are the existing path, and there is no second "button answer" code path to keep in sync. A typed "2" coerces to the same canonical text; anything else passes through untouched as a new instruction. **(3) APPROVE/DENY** — per-chat opt-in via `/approvals` (**DEFAULT OFF**: an un-opted chat is byte-identical to before — `surface:'autonomous'`, ungranted mutation default-denies, never stalls). Opted in, the chat runs `surface:'interactive'` and the broker pauses on a keyboard carrying its own once/session/always/deny vocabulary. The HOST keeps the pause/resolve (`channelAskConsent`/`channelResolveConsent` over the existing unit-tested `consentwait.js` — same CONSENT_TIMEOUT_MS floor, same instant deny on abort); the hub owns only the render and the button→decision hop. Deliberately a SEPARATE map from `pendingByRun`: the browser's consent card resolves by a runId it never has for a channel run, so sharing it would surface prompts through `/api/state/snapshot` that the app is structurally unable to answer. **(4) COMMAND MENU SYNC** — one `COMMANDS` table now drives `parseCommand`, `/help` AND `setMyCommands`, so Telegram's blue "/" menu cannot drift from what the hub implements. **ADDITIVE THROUGHOUT — no `shared/events.js` change:** `permission.prompt`/`permission.response` already carry this, and the docs claiming a clarify keyboard "would need a new rung + a clarify tool/loop seam" (MESSAGING_POLISH_BACKLOG §3) were wrong on both counts — `brief.ask` already IS the clarify tool. Every button dep is optional, so Discord/Slack/Matrix/Signal keep the numbered-text rendering byte-identically (`wireChannel` passes none of them => `buttonsOk=false`). **LIVE-PROVEN ON REAL TELEGRAM** (@bottest173bot) via a logging proxy in front of api.telegram.org plus a mock provider — real wire, full visibility both directions, zero spend. Confirmed with ZERO 400s: `setMyCommands` (the call Telegram rejects WHOLESALE if any name/description is invalid); `sendMessage` with `reply_markup` as a NESTED JSON object (correct because we POST application/json — the string-encoding requirement is form-encoded only); `editMessageText` with `message_id` as a STRING, which Telegram coerces exactly as it already does for `chat_id`; `answerCallbackQuery`; `callback_data` returning at 15-16 bytes; and the REAL `callback_query` shape matching `telegram.js normalize()` field-for-field — that parser had NEVER run against real Telegram before (it sat behind the noop), making it the single biggest unknown in the lane. Approve/deny walked end to end: the run PAUSED at `fs.write`, the keyboard rendered, the tap RESUMED it, and `notes.md` landed on disk containing "hello" — the approval had teeth, not just the look of it. **DEFECTS FOUND IN THIS LANE'S OWN WORK:** `sendConsentPrompt` is fire-and-forget, so a keyboard that FAILED to send retired its token and left the already-armed waiter with nothing to resolve — the run then sat the full CONSENT_TIMEOUT_MS before failing closed, strictly worse than the autonomous floor the chat opted IN from; now denies immediately (same decision, no wait), guarded by a test that asserts on TIMING because a regression still ends in 'deny' and only the wait distinguishes correct from broken. And BOTH new test files ended in a bare `console.log` instead of `A.report()`, so a failed assertion printed FAIL and still exited 0 — the gate could not have caught a regression in either; fixed and verified in both directions by deliberately reverting the stall fix (98 ok/exit 0 with it, 2 problems/exit 1 without). The live walk then caught one thing no test would have: the toast read `✓ ✅ Allow for this session`, a doubled tick from prefixing a label that already carries its own marker. **TWO REFERENCE-HARNESS BUGS FOUND WHILE MINING AND DELIBERATELY NOT PORTED:** its `resolve_gateway_approval` pops the session queue HEAD, so with two concurrent prompts a tap on the second answers the first (we key by promptId, making that unrepresentable); and its four callback-state dicts have no eviction, so an ignored keyboard leaks forever (we bound at 200). **TRAPS:** a bay routing plan projects PURE room objects with NO baseline, so `objects:['cabinet']` alone yields "no compute — place a computer in the room"; the Task Brief gate blocks every consequential tool until `brief_proceed` (objective >=4 chars), so any e2e driving a real write must script that call FIRST; a bare e2e workspace offers ONLY the consent-free skill tools, so a consent test there silently proves nothing until a cabinet is projected; and the station fires its own background runs (skill review, quest refresh) against the same mock provider, so a positional script queue gets eaten — key mock steps by the request's LAST USER MESSAGE. Also: `deliver()` now returns `{ok,messageId,text}` instead of a bool (a keyboard needs the message id to edit) — no caller read the old return, but re-check after any merge. Merge conflicts: `package.json` ONLY (unioned both sides' `test:http:raw` entries, trunk order canonical, strict-JSON + no-BOM verified). `sidecar/channels/hub.js` auto-merged INSIDE the very block both sides edited — trunk's `groundedFor` grounded-suggestion path and this lane's keyboard registration both survived and were verified by eye plus symbol-count; `sidecar/index.js` verified for duplicate/dropped defs (auto-merge lies there). Gate: **test:fast 385/385 + FULL test:http green on the merged tree BEFORE the trunk merge**, which then fast-forwarded. OPEN: `/approvals on` is silently swallowed when sent while the bot is DOWN — drop-pending-on-connect correctly discards stale backlog, but on a chat with no owner claimed yet it does not even get the "I was offline, N messages arrived" notice, so a first-time user sees pure silence (pre-existing, hit TWICE during this lane's live walk, chip-worthy); MarkdownV2 formatting, outbound media (`sendPhoto`/`sendDocument`), the `/model` picker keyboard, message reactions and the `(i/N)` chunk indicator all remain unbuilt (deliberately out of scope — Andrew scoped this lane to buttons + command sync). NOT PUSHED (trunk still carries the previous merge's unpushed `website/` changes, which auto-deploy starnetos.com on push — Andrew's call, unchanged by this lane, which touches no website file).
- 2026-07-25: FIRST-RUN SILENCE — branch `claude/starnet-telegram-improvements-7fb658` (534aa968) -> trunk merge `ed6979c1` (snapshot `80b17697`, fast-forward). Surfaced by the inline-keyboard live walk rather than by any test: `/approvals on` was sent to a bot that was DOWN, discarded on connect, and only worked when resent — twice in one session. Message a stopped bot and you got PURE SILENCE, which is indistinguishable from a broken bot, at exactly the moment a new user is deciding whether the thing works. **Drop-pending-on-connect is correct and unchanged** — a restart must never replay stale directives, and that is the whole point of `getUpdates({offset:-1})` confirm-and-discard. The gap was the APOLOGY: it only fired for chats whose owner was ALREADY known (`admit` requires `!!owner && userId === owner`), and a fresh install has no owner yet — so the very first contact, the one case where a human has no idea what happened, was the one case that got nothing. **We still cannot apologise at connect**, for two independent reasons: claiming ownership from stale backlog is precisely the anti-stale-directive behaviour being preserved (so we cannot call `ownerOk()`, which claims as a side effect), and answering a chat we have NOT yet proven is the owner would break owner-only silence toward a stranger who found a discoverable bot. So the notice is **DEFERRED**: unclaimed-owner DM backlog is recorded in a bounded `deferredOfflineNotice` set (cap 50, so a flood of stranger DMs while the bot was down cannot grow it), held, and paid ONCE when that chat proves it is the owner by getting admitted — fired detached and best-effort in `dispatch` so it can never delay or block the real message. A stranger is still never answered, even after someone else claims. **ALSO REMOVED A FABRICATED COUNT** (found while fixing, same sentence): the notice said "N messages arrived while I was away", but `getUpdates({offset:-1})` returns only the LAST pending update while advancing past it discards every earlier one too — so N counted what we could SEE, not what we DROPPED, and confidently under-reported (observed live: `pending_update_count:2`, notice would have claimed 1). It now states no number and asks for a resend, which is true whatever the real figure was; one shared `OFFLINE_NOTICE` constant for both the immediate and deferred paths so they cannot drift. The two pinned count assertions in `channels.adapter.test.js` were updated to assert the honest copy plus an explicit `!/\d+ messages? arrived/` guard against the claim coming back. New coverage: withheld while nobody has claimed (poll parked so nothing can be admitted), paid exactly once on the message that claims and NOT per message, and never sent to a stranger even after the owner claims. **The guard was proven to fail** — disabling the deferred send gives 1 problem + exit 1. Gate: **test:fast 386/386 + FULL test:http green on the merged tree before the trunk merge**, which then fast-forwarded. Trunk's advance since the previous merge was `qa/STATUS.md` only — no code overlap, no conflicts. NOT PUSHED (trunk still carries earlier unpushed `website/` changes that auto-deploy starnetos.com on push — Andrew's call, untouched by this lane).
- 2026-07-25: STATION BACKDROPS — branch `claude/starnet-background-variations-6dab1b` (23 commits) -> trunk merge `2bf21800` (snapshot `dc594624`, fast-forward). The station could only ever float in one sky; it now has four, picked in SETTINGS > APPEARANCE > BACKDROP and saved station-wide on `settings.backdrop`. **THE VOID stays the default and is byte-identical to what shipped** — proven by loading the pre-change module alongside the new one and diffing ImageData at three resolutions (0 differing px, max channel delta 0). Andrew stripped a galaxy and planets out of the default on 2026-07-14 (514386de) and that call still stands: richer space is opt-in, never bolted onto the default. **Framework:** `SpaceBG.draw` now takes the world camera so finite-distance layers can parallax; world.js draws the backdrop AFTER the camAnim/camLock/camLerp settle block (sampling before it left every layer a frame behind the station); the tile cache is keyed on backdrop id, not size alone, or switching at the same canvas size silently keeps the old tiles; `tile2` snaps offsets to whole pixels so a pixel-art tile never lands on a half-pixel. **THE NURSERY** (a structured emission nebula in a deep field) replaced an earlier DEEP FIELD that was, accurately, "the void with planets" — it reused the default's own recipe and re-instated art that had already been rejected on its merits. **OCEAN** and **NIGHT CITY** float over open water and a lit grid. **LAWS THIS LANE ESTABLISHED, all measured rather than eyeballed:** (1) a backdrop must sit at ~0.5-0.65 of the STATION's luma — OCEAN first shipped at 1.25, brighter than the station, which inverts the composition and makes the station read as a cutout pasted on; final: void 0.53 / nursery 0.54 / ocean 0.66 / city 0.63. (2) A surface is a CONTINUOUS field, never sparse bright marks on dark — OCEAN v1 drew the sea as scattered points and gave the glint the same code shape as the star twinkle, so it read as a starfield ("it looks like stars"); measured pop-out 2.41 against the void's 5.43, rebuilt to 1.53. (3) Smooth noise renders as BLUR; quantize, and for soft volumes DITHER with a per-pixel hash rather than an ordered matrix — a Bayer 4x4 computed at half res and upscaled put a visible cross-hatch mesh over the nebula (phase-luma spread 2.10, now 0.123, below the void's un-dithered 0.623). (4) Thin gas must be NOTHING, not a veil — an `alpha = 0.10 + 0.90*t` floor tinted 62% of the frame and left no clean sky anywhere. (5) **A backdrop with a legible SUBJECT needs a FIXED TILE.** Tiles are canvas-sized and laid out in normalised coords, so every resize re-lays them — invisible for a starfield, but expanding COMMS grew a completely different nebula and blew out to white at the new aspect. `fixedTile()` plus the new `tileN()` mean a resize reveals more of the SAME sky (100% pixel-identical across the two COMMS shapes); the per-resize build cost became a once-per-session cost as a side effect. **A FOREST ground layer was built and then CUT by Andrew** (aca9f6c3 + 3e7b6b53, reverted in 79705937 with the four skies restored byte-identical); its lesson is worth keeping — ground at the station's plane belongs in WORLD space between setTransform and the bake and must ZOOM with the camera, the exact opposite of what a backdrop must do. **TRAPS:** editing any `frontend/app/*.js` or `frontend/css/*` requires a claims release-surface re-lock AS ITS OWN COMMIT (the gate asserts candidateCommit != sourceCommit) or test:fast dies at step 151 with errors that read like QA infrastructure rot rather than your edit; `website/app/**` is a GENERATED mirror gated in test:fast, so a frontend change needs `npm run sync:website` — caught by the gate on the sync merge, not before. **MEASUREMENT TRAPS THAT COST REAL TIME:** canvas `drawImage` is dispatched to the GPU asynchronously, so synchronous per-frame timings are meaningless — 8.58ms and then 7.93ms were reported as facts and were artifacts (a bare four-copy blit benchmarks at 0.01ms by the same method); "optimizing" for that phantom cost brightened the nebula from 30 to 54.5 luma and had to be reverted wholesale. Synchronous CPU work IS measurable, but single shots swing 2x (118ms and 223ms from identical code) — take a median of five. And three separate metrics gave confident wrong answers purely by being computed in the wrong frame: a "ground" sample box that was actually inside the station bake, pixel flatness measured at 2.5x zoom where the upscale duplicates every pixel (both versions read 100%), and an "empty sky" test that counted the base fill colour as nebula haze and so hid an alpha floor for a whole round. Sanity-check any new visual metric against THE VOID, whose look is settled, before trusting a number from it. Gate: **test:fast 386/386 green ON TRUNK** (the merge was a fast-forward so the trunk tree is byte-identical to the gated lane head; re-run on trunk anyway). Live-verified at 1440x900 via CDP: all four backdrops reach the real station canvas, world.js hands the backdrop a real camera (intercepted argument, 55 calls) which moves after a synthetic drag-pan, and a save holding a removed id resolves to void rather than blanking the sky. NOT PUSHED — this merge touches `website/`, which auto-deploys starnetos.com on push; that remains Andrew's call, as it already was for the two merges before this one. OPEN: OCEAN has never had Andrew's eyes on its finished state (he flagged its lighting mid-build and the fix went in unreviewed).
- floor+wall materials lane -> trunk 64180a77 · test:fast 386/386 green · deck AND wall are now hue x material (4 new decks, 6 new wall materials incl. VIEWPORT = a real hole to the live starfield); fixed the unclipped hull rim over erased chamfer corners, collapsed a corner's seven curves into one integer raster, made the corner's inner boundary an ELLIPSE so it lands on two straight walls of different depths, and moved the chamfer pass before bakeRoomLighting so a corner is lit like every other surface. New crtlab knobs: deckSeam, wallDetail. GUARDIAN GOLDENS WILL ALL SHIFT — walls and decks changed on every station; re-bless from the pin.
- turf/hedge colour pass -> trunk 837cc964 · test:fast 389/389 green · U.shade lerps toward WHITE so every lightening step desaturates (fern: sat 0.43 -> 0.06 by +0.6), which made the brightest grass blades the greyest pixels on the deck.
