- 2026-07-25: BROWSER CDP PORT NO LONGER SELF-IDENTIFIES AS AUTOMATION (branch `claude/headed-browser-update-445796` -> trunk merge `6e418f03`; test:fast 390 + test:http green ON TRUNK). The production launch passed `--remote-debugging-port=0`; Chromium's `content/child/runtime_features.cc` special-cases port 0 and calls `EnableAutomationControlled(true)`, so EVERY launch set `navigator.webdriver`. Google documents that it refuses sign-in to browsers "being controlled through software automation" (support.google.com/accounts/answer/7675428) — i.e. the attended-login takeover (`browser.login`) was flagging itself to the exact wall it exists to get the Commander through, and the failure would have read as "the login feature is broken" rather than "one launch arg". `cdpPort:0` still MEANS "give this run its own private endpoint" (a process-wide port lets one agent run attach to another's browser); we now honour that by allocating a non-zero ephemeral port ourselves — bind 127.0.0.1:0, keep what the OS hands us, release, pass the number — instead of delegating allocation to Chromium. Same per-run isolation, no automation tell. LIVE A/B ON REAL HEADED CHROME: old launch -> `navigator.webdriver === true`; new launch -> `false`, on a self-allocated port, clean `Chrome/149` UA with no "for Testing" string. TRAP THAT COST THE FIRST ATTEMPT: keeping the `DevToolsActivePort` read-back "for confirmation" made the driver unable to attach AT ALL — installed Chrome launched on an EXPLICIT port may never write that file (Chromium writes it when IT picks the port), and the symptom was a silent 40-iteration retry with ZERO fetch attempts because the `readFileSync` threw inside the same `try` that wraps the fetch. Once we choose the port, a successful `/json/list` on our own port IS the readiness proof; the file read had to go. `test/browser.test.js` previously LOCKED `--remote-debugging-port=0` and was rewritten into the fingerprint regression guard (asserts a real non-zero port AND that it is not the 9347 process-wide default, so the isolation property that motivated port 0 stays covered) — browser.test now 122 assertions. SCOPE HONESTY: `--headless=new` sets the same flag independently, so this cleans up the HEADED login window specifically — which is where sign-in happens; headless research runs still report webdriver true. It removes one DOCUMENTED signal; Google does not publish its full signal list, so this is not a promise that sign-in now succeeds. Small new race accepted: the port is held for microseconds between allocation and Chrome binding it; a steal fails loudly with "could not attach" rather than silently (Puppeteer avoids this by using port 0 — exactly the trade being reversed). STILL OPEN: the real-site login walk has NEVER been run (no site picked), and the wider browser-parity gap is audit-only — the agent browser scores ~6.5/15 vs the 2026 harness table-stakes set: new page targets are actively KILLED (`Target.closeTarget`, browser.js:376, locked by a test) so popups/`_blank`/SSO-popup flows dead-end, the entire wait vocabulary is three fixed sleeps with ZERO settle after click (stale-DOM corrupter), the Network/Emulation/DOM/Storage CDP domains are never enabled (a 403 is indistinguishable from a blank page), and `screenshot()` is write-only — its single consumer is `vision()`, which returns a byte COUNT and discards the PNG, so the user can never see what the agent saw.
- 2026-07-25: AGENT CONFIG DOCS WROTE TO THE WRONG AGENT — `claude/agent-context-config-erase-05e2f7` -> trunk merge `f509e77f` (snapshot `10cdfd94`), re-lock `79429535`. From a v0.6.6 user report: "when I add context to my agents in their config settings, I always save it and it always erases to zero." The dossier's four markdown editors were the ONE per-agent control that never named its target: every neighbour on that panel already passed an agentId (setAgentModelPin / setAgentPersona / setAgentApproval / setAgentWorkshop / setAgentName / setAgentSkin) but `access.config.apply({[key]: val})` did not, so `applyAgentConfig` wrote to the module-scope FOCUSED agent — whoever COMMS happened to be on — and the card then repainted from `present[sel]`, the agent actually on screen, whose doc was never touched. On a freshly summoned specialist (purpose/context/manual all empty) that repaint reads "0 chars": the erase. TWO failures per save, not one — the edit was lost AND the focused agent's own doc was silently overwritten, with the toast still claiming it landed. It ran both directions (a specialist focused + the hero's dossier open wrote to the specialist). SOLO STATIONS ARE IMMUNE (hero focused + hero's dossier = correct target), which is how it shipped and why this class must be reproduced WITH A CREW. `applyAgentConfig(patch, agentId)` now resolves the target; agentId is OPTIONAL and defaults to focused, so the awakening's commit, deploySpecialty, the marketplace recruit path and App.applyConfig from the slash suite are unchanged. Live side effects (Chat.setSystem, Voice.init, syncChannels, Harness.setModel/setReasoningEffort, the gt-model HUD) are gated on the target being focused — mirroring setAgentPersona/setAgentModelPin — while pushRoster + persist always run. **SECOND BUG, CREATED BY THE FIRST FIX, found by auditing rather than by a test:** `rosterRole()` reads a crew member's specialty/purpose and the lead's `YOUR CREW: <name> — <role>` line is baked into its STORED systemPrompt, so once a SPECIALIST's purpose could finally move through this path the orchestrator went stale — briefing itself, and the sidecar, on a job its specialist no longer had (proven live: the crew line did not move on save). `recomposeOrchestrators()` now runs when a patch re-purposes a non-orchestrator, before pushRoster; that also closes the same staleness on `deploySpecialty` (sets specialtyId then patches {purpose,manual}), which was PRE-EXISTING and not introduced here. LAW: anything that can change a crew member's name, purpose or specialtyId is a roster-change point and must recompose the orchestrators — roster-clause.test §D already locked summon/rehydrate/rename; applyAgentConfig is the fourth. TRAPS: `serializeAgentLite` does NOT persist systemPrompt (derived, recomposed on rehydrate), so asserting a crew member's prompt against localStorage reads undefined and looks like a failure — check the live object, or `<workspace>/agent.roster.json`, which is the real proof a doc reaches a run; and `test/slash.config.test.js` needles applyAgentConfig internals by literal (`agent.reasoningEffort = eff`), so renaming a local inside a shipped frontend function breaks a gate. NEW `test/agent-config-target.test.js` (36 assertions, in fast.list) extracts applyAgentConfig from source and EXECUTES it against a stub station whose composeSystemPrompt models the lead's crew clause — targeting, bystander isolation, the no-id/unknown-id fallbacks, all four doc keys, the focus-gated side effects, and the recompose; it fails against the shipped v0.6.6 tree, and stripping the recompose line turns section G red (3 failures). Merge conflict: `claims.json` only (both sides had re-locked) — resolved to trunk's copy with the real lock stamped at the merge commit; `test/fast.list` auto-merged and was union-verified (392 steps, nothing dropped or duplicated). Gate: **test:fast 392 + FULL test:http green ON TRUNK**; live-verified pre-merge on a seeded two-agent station (specialist card 0 -> 25 chars, each agent keeps its own doc across a page reload, agent.roster.json carries each agent's own doc AND the lead's refreshed crew line). NOT live-re-verified post-merge. **NOT PUSHED** — the diff touches `website/` (generated mirror), and pushing `feat/harness-backend` auto-deploys starnetos.com; that is Andrew's call. Noted-not-fixed: `patch.model` sets a.model without a.provider (pre-existing shape; only focused-path callers pass model, and the dossier's model card uses setModel, which sets both).
- 2026-07-27: AN AGENT THE OVERSEER IS ASKED TO CREATE ARRIVES WITH ITS OWN DESK — branch `claude/lucid-elgamal-986d26` (6 commits, `eb06cdc0`..`d297c625`) -> trunk merge `ef4cd806`, re-lock `85cfde86` (reset point `c99a3b10`, NOT the original snapshot `b98e1643` — the COMMS sound-board lane merged mid-baseline and a reset to the older snapshot would have discarded it). From Andrew: ask the overseer for a new agent and it minted the identity, sprite and workstream but no WORKSTATION, so the specialist stood on bare deck, never walked to a desk, and the Commander was left to build one by hand. **THE RULE HAS TWO SIDES AND BOTH ARE NOW GUARDED:** an agent the overseer was ASKED to create arrives with a desk bound to IT; an agent the Commander creates BY HAND in the Recruitment Bay does NOT — that path is deliberately untouched (new agent's chat + the PLACE ITS DESK chip, they choose the spot). `summonAgent(spec, opts)` gained `opts.desk`; ONLY `summonForRequest` (the `crew.summon.request` handler) passes it. This is not a general prop-placement power: one desk, one path, for the agent being created. **`ensureWorkstation` was ALREADY the seeder** (it plants the hero's starter desk) and was reused rather than duplicated; it now prefers a spot with a free seat tile IN FRONT (a desk whose south row is walled is a desk nobody can sit at — `canPlaceProp` alone will happily wall one in), keeps crew desks on the hero's own desk row instead of tucking them under the north wall face the tall-walls bake raises at y1, and spills into another room when the spawn room is packed instead of stranding the agent deskless. **ADOPT BEFORE BUILDING:** `deleteAgent` UNBINDS a removed specialist's props and never demolishes them (correct — that floor is the Commander's), so a build-only seeder turned summon->delete->summon into a pile of abandoned desks; the seeder now takes over an unbound workstation in the SPAWN ROOM first (an unbound capability prop is dead furniture — `mayTouchProp` means no agent may walk to an unassigned one). Spawn-room only, deliberately: adopting a free desk in some far lab would exile a new specialist's whole zone/leash to a room nobody chose. Also fixed a self-inflicted one found in review — `summonForRequest` re-called `ensureWorkstation` to read back where the desk landed, and that call can PLACE, after `summonAgent`'s `persist()`; it is a pure `propsByAgent` read now. **LAW: never re-call a SEEDER to read back what it did.** The desk location rides the ACK (`/api/summon/ack` gained an optional `desk`; `summonRequest` resolves `{agentId, desk}` and `team.summon` reads `.agentId` while still tolerating a bare id string), so the tool states the room from the floor instead of assuming it and a failed seed claims nothing. **THE DESK CHANGES NO CAPABILITY PAYLOAD** — `heroCaps`/`stationCaps` both skip `computer` (compute is the harness freebie), measured byte-identical across a summon, and a desk never enters the RoutingPlan (`pipeline.js` compiles `if (p.t !== 'bay') continue`) — so tool reach and skill availability cannot drift. **NEW: `npm run qa:summon-delegate`** (`scripts/qa/summon-delegate-journey.mjs`, NOT in any gate — needs a real Chrome) drives the whole Commander sentence: real sidecar + real browser + a mock model walking the lead through `brief.proceed -> team.summon -> team.dispatch`, then asserts the new agent works SEATED at its own desk, and immediately after runs the BAY's own call shape on the SAME floor to assert it seeds nothing — a guard proving only the seeding half would let the bay start seeding and stay green. **THE CHAIN NEEDS `brief.proceed` FIRST** or team.summon returns `ERROR: Task Brief gate:` and no agent is created — a mock that skips it looks exactly like a broken feature. **The delegated pose comes from `handoff()`** (tool_call `team.dispatch` sets `delegateLead`, the worker's `agent.run.start` fires the handoff), NOT from the run trigger — the `trigger!=='directive'` serverLit listener excludes delegated workers by design and the snapshot reconcile is 30s, so neither poses them; before this lane `deskPropFor` returned null and `sitting` was forced false, which is why `working && sitting` is the honest before/after. **MEASUREMENT TRAPS THAT COST TWO FALSE "BROKEN" READINGS:** 400ms CDP polling ALIASES past the pose window and reported "never worked" on a run that provably did (record poses IN-PAGE at frame rate); and world tile = LOCAL tile **+** `geo.origin` (origin is negative, e.g. `{tx:-3,ty:-3}`) — subtracting puts the body outside the station and reads as a miss. Also: a tool RESULT inside a request body is ESCAPED JSON, so parse `messages[].content` rather than regexing the raw body. Merge conflict: `claims.json` ONLY (both sides had re-locked) — resolved to trunk's copy (182 files, the correct path set) with the real lock stamped at the merge commit as its own commit; pathSetSha256 unchanged. Hotfiles `worldmodel.js` / `orchestration.js` / `package.json` auto-merged and were verified per ritual: `node --check` clean on all seven touched JS files, every summon/desk symbol defined exactly once, package.json strict-JSON with no BOM. No `shared/events.js` change, no credential movement. Gate: **test:fast 402/402 + FULL test:http green ON TRUNK after the merge**, and `qa:summon-delegate` re-run ON MERGED TRUNK 18/18 (the feature still does the real thing after 50 commits of other lanes). **TRUNK BASELINE TRAP:** the pre-merge baseline came back RED on `qa-product-perfect-claims.test.js` and was a PHANTOM — the same test passes standalone; a concurrent run in the tree produces it (see the two-concurrent-runs law). Always re-baseline before believing trunk is broken. NOT PUSHED — the diff touches `website/` (generated mirror) and pushing `feat/harness-backend` auto-deploys starnetos.com; Andrew's call, as with the merges before it. OPEN, pre-existing and unrelated to the desk: `workerFoot()` fans out only around other SUMMONED bodies, so a freshly summoned body can materialise on the hero's exact tile for a beat before it walks off (cosmetic; `containBody` re-homes it).
- 2026-07-27: THE COMMS COMPOSER HINT CLIPPED OFF THE ONLY POINTER TO THE SLASH PALETTE — branch `claude/comms-menu-audit-84bae0` (2 commits, `a50d1cdc`..`d980c7e4`) -> trunk merge `6613b3f4`, re-lock `3ffd6ffb` (snapshot/reset point `eaa5d8e6`). From a COMMS UX audit driven live against `dev/seed-mock-comms.js`: the composer placeholder `speak to your agent… ( / for commands )` MEASURES 250px in VT323 16px, but the textarea's inner text budget at the DEFAULT COMMS width is ~217px, so it rendered as `speak to your agent… ( / for` — the clip lands exactly on the words that explain `/`, and that parenthetical is the ONLY in-app hint that the 43-command slash palette (`sidecar/slash.js`) exists. Now `speak to your agent · / commands` (205px, **22px margin measured live in a clean browser profile**, `clipped:false`), which also matches the palette's own `/ COMMANDS` header. **SEND CANNOT GIVE THE SPACE BACK:** `index.html` claimed SEND "collapses to just the glyph on a narrow panel", but `.cs-label` has NO CSS rule anywhere and `.chat-send svg` is `display:none` with the brackets drawn by `::before`/`::after` — hiding the label renders an empty `[ ]`. The stale comment is why nobody re-checked the narrow case after the ROBCO restyle; a PLACEHOLDER WIDTH LAW comment now sits on the element with the budget written down. **TWO SITES OWN THE STRING** — the `index.html` attribute AND the `endInterview()` reset in `chat.js`; fixing only the first would have restored the clipped copy the moment onboarding ended. Mirrored to `website/app` via `sync:website`. **THE GATE LIED WHILE THE EDIT WAS UNCOMMITTED:** `buildReleaseSurface` hashes files AT A COMMIT, so the first `test:fast` was a false green against the old blob; committing exposed 9 real failures in `qa-product-perfect-claims.test.js`. Re-run the gate AFTER committing, always. Merge conflict: `claims.json` ONLY (both sides had re-locked, and trunk's `pathSetSha256` differed — trunk's path set had grown to 182) — resolved to trunk's copy with the real lock stamped at the merge commit as its own commit; pathSetSha256 unchanged. No hotfile in the diff; `node --check` clean on both touched JS files, no stray conflict markers. No `shared/events.js` change, no credential movement. Gate: **test:fast 402/402 GREEN ON TRUNK after the merge**. `test:http` NOT run — the diff is frontend + qa ledger only, no sidecar/route/ship file touched. NOT PUSHED — the diff touches `website/` (generated mirror); Andrew's call, as with the merges before it. PRE-EXISTING AND UNRELATED: Green Guardian sits RED on trunk with 1 open finding (its in-flight `qa/STATUS.md` row update was left uncommitted in the tree and deliberately NOT swept into this merge). ALSO FOUND, NOT FIXED (deliberately — Andrew scoped this to the placeholder alone): the bare-`/` palette shows only 8 of 43 commands with no "N more" affordance (`matchCommands` returns `all.slice(0, 8)`), so fixing the hint now points more people at an incomplete list.
- 2026-07-27: "FULL ACCESS" ON A FOLDER CARD STOPPED MEANING ANYTHING — branch `claude/full-access-permission-loop-e3038a` (2 commits, `facca734`..`084cb594`) -> trunk merge `d042e0cd`, re-lock `210e9bfa` (snapshot/reset point `6b66af18`). SECOND instance of the repeat-approval defect the connector gate hit the same day (`418a26a4`): a user with Full Access on could not get routines working, and clicked "Full access" **five times** on a card that kept returning. Root cause was NOT the connector gate and NOT the consent broker — it was `sidecar/pathtrust.js`, the NS-5 folder-trust card ("work in <root>?"). `guard()` handled the decision `full` by falling through to the `once` branch — allow THIS access, record nothing — while `always` blessed the root and never asked again. So the cards **strongest** button was the only one that bought nothing, and the next file touch in the SAME folder asked again. **THE CLASS IS THREE PLACES, NOT TWO:** a grade can be dropped in `inputpolicy.js` (run authority, per-call), `permissions.js` (the broker), AND `pathtrust.js` (the folder boundary) — the connector fix closed one and was recorded as closing the class. Swept all three this lane: every registered capability (`cabinet jukebox mcp: memory orchestrator physical-input quest studio taskbrief toolsearch visible-desktop web workbench`) is classified in `impactOfTool`, so no shipped tool reaches the one-shot re-ask path; both `makeConsentBroker` call sites pass `grantsBlanket`; both decision doors (`/api/consent` at index.js:9790, `channelResolveConsent` at 2105) pass `full` through verbatim. **MEASURED, not reasoned** — a probe booting a REAL sidecar with an `approvalMode:"full"` agent doing 5 reads in one project folder: **5 cards before, 1 after**, with a standing revocable `path:<root>` grant where there were none; re-run against the merged trunk bytes, still 1. Both new suites verified RED against the pre-fix module first (`after "full", read #2 raises NO card — expected 0, got 1`) — a consent test that passes both ways proves nothing. The old comments reasoning ("a directory bless is never a side effect of a blanket capability grant") is TRUE and UNCHANGED — pathtrust still never reads `grantsBlanket`/`*` — it was applied to the wrong value: `decision` is not a leaked grant, it is the direct answer to a card naming that exact folder. Hardline `.env`/`.git` floor, symlink re-proof and THE UNATTENDED RULE (a cron run still hard-denies, never prompts) all untouched; `once` still grants nothing standing; a `full` whose persist fails still DENIES. Conflict: `claims.json` ONLY and exactly the two expected fields (both sides had re-locked); neither sides hash describes the MERGED `chat.js`, so resolved to trunks copy and re-locked at the merge commit as its own commit. `chat.js` auto-merged — both sides verified present by grep, `node --check` clean on all three touched JS files. No hotfile, no `shared/events.js`/`shared/schema.js`, no credential movement. Gate: **test:fast 402/402 + FULL test:http green ON TRUNK**, claims 64/64. **NOT PUSHED** — the diff touches `website/` (generated mirror) and `deploy-website.yml` fires on push for those paths, so pushing auto-deploys starnetos.com; Andrews call. **STILL OPEN (product-semantics, Andrews call):** the Full Access POSTURE (`approvalMode:"full"`, or the machine-wide `SKYNET_FULL_ACCESS` env) does not reach pathtrust at all — `runPathTrust` (index.js:8699) passes no bypass — so a full-access agent now gets exactly ONE folder card per NEW project root instead of a loop, not zero. Deliberate: `shell.exec` is jailed too (refuses `..`, `C:\`, UNC), so the folder card is the ONLY real filesystem-reach boundary left. PRE-EXISTING AND UNRELATED: Green Guardian still RED on trunk with 1 open P1 (`a735d29f`, audit `log/frozen-bus`); its in-flight dashboard row was stashed out of this merge and restored untouched, not swept in.
